**_i_**
### **FOREWORD**

The rapid advancement of Artificial Intelligence (AI) has profoundly impacted every facet of
human life, reshaping industries, transforming businesses, and redefining the global economic
landscape. At the intersection of AI and entrepreneurship lies a unique and powerful synergy
that offers unprecedented opportunities for innovation, efficiency, and financial success. This
edited volume, **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship: Strategies for Financial Success in Startups_**
**_and Economies,_** serves as a timely exploration of this transformative paradigm.

In an era characterized by relentless technological evolution, startups and entrepreneurs are
uniquely positioned to leverage AI to create competitive advantages. From predictive
analytics and customer insights to operational optimization and personalized solutions, the
potential of AI is vast and multifaceted. This book delves into these possibilities, offering a
multidisciplinary perspective on how entrepreneurs can harness AI to achieve sustainable
growth and navigate the complexities of modern markets.

The contributions to this volume come from a diverse group of academicians, research
scholars, and social scientists across various disciplines, including Commerce, Economics,
and Management. Each chapter embodies a commitment to originality and rigor, presenting
unpublished research, articles, and case studies that illuminate the theoretical and practical
dimensions of AI-driven entrepreneurship. The breadth of perspectives ensures that this book
not only addresses the needs of entrepreneurs but also provides valuable insights for
policymakers, educators, and researchers seeking to understand the economic implications of
AI.

As editors, Professors Pradipta Banerjee and Dr. Narendra Kumar have curated a collection
that bridges the gap between academic inquiry and practical application. Their vision
underscores the book’s dual aim: to inspire entrepreneurs to embrace the opportunities
presented by AI and to empower them with strategies to overcome its challenges. By doing
so, the volume aspires to contribute to a global entrepreneurial ecosystem that is both
innovative and inclusive.

This book arrives at a critical juncture when businesses worldwide are grappling with
uncertainty and seeking resilience through technological adoption. It is my belief that the
insights presented here will act as a beacon of guidance, enabling entrepreneurs to unlock the
immense potential of AI while driving financial success and fostering economic development.
Moreover, I commend the editors and contributors for their dedication to this endeavor and
invite readers to immerse themselves in this enriching body of work.

**Ganga Prasad Prasain**
Tripura University (A Central University)

Suryamaninagar - 799022

Tripura, India

**_ii_**
### **PREFACE**

It is a matter of great pleasure and extreme privilege for us to place before the valued and
learned academicians, professionals, researchers, and students the edited book titled ‘AIDriven Entrepreneurship: Strategies for Financial Success in Startups and Economies’. The
transformative power of Artificial Intelligence (AI) has redefined the entrepreneurial
landscape of the globe, posing challenges as well as fostering unprecedented opportunities.
As startups and economies strive to navigate an era of rapid technological evolution, AI
emerges as a key enabler for innovation, operational efficiency, and financial success, both in
the short term and the long term. Recognizing this shift, the present volume aims to explore
the intricate relationship between AI and entrepreneurship through a multidisciplinary lens
and is expected to serve as a repository of cutting-edge research, insights, and case studies,
shedding light on the multidimensional opportunities and challenges presented by AI-driven
entrepreneurship.

The book brings together an array of unpublished and original research papers, articles, and
case studies from a diverse cohort of academicians, research scholars, corporate leaders, and
social scientists from disciplines like Commerce, Economics, and Management, aiming to
curate a holistic compendium that delves into the theme of AI-driven entrepreneurship from
multidisciplinary perspectives. From strategy formulation to financial planning, the book
addresses key aspects of leveraging AI for startup success, while also examining its broader
implications for the economic development of nations around the globe. The response to this
initiative was overwhelming, with high-quality submissions from various parts of the world,
including Spain, Nepal, and a multitude of academic institutions and professional bodies
across India. The resulting contributions represent a wide array of theoretical frameworks,
empirical analyses, and practical insights into different dimensions of the focal theme of the
book, making this piece of work a comprehensive resource for both academic and
professional readers.

Besides, the chapters included in this volume underscore the transformative potential of AI in
entrepreneurship. They examine its applications across various domains, including social
media, human capital management, financial risk mitigation, digital marketing, healthcare,
and niche areas, such as rural tourism and online gambling platforms. These studies highlight
the diverse ways AI tools and strategies are being leveraged to enhance operational efficiency,
optimize costs, mitigate risks, and unlock new opportunities for innovation and growth. The
topics covered reflect the broad applicability of AI in driving financial success and fostering
resilience in startups, as well as its role in addressing challenges in specific economic and
cultural contexts.

The research works in this volume explore how AI is influencing entrepreneurial strategies in
both emerging economies and established markets. The contributions examine its impact on
payment ecosystems, its ability to drive innovation in financial technology, and its role in
redefining workforce management through automation and data analytics. Furthermore, the
studies delve into the integration of AI in formulating digital marketing strategies, providing
tools and insights for startups that enable them to enhance their market presence and customer
engagement. Additionally, the volume offers perspectives on how AI is shaping the tourism
sector, healthcare services, and even ethical considerations surrounding its applications in
industries like online gambling.

The diversity of the contributions in this volume not only underscores the pervasive influence
of AI but also highlights the ingenuity of researchers and practitioners in harnessing the

**_iii_**

potential of AI to solve complex challenges and capitalize on emerging opportunities. The
contributors, hailing from varied geographical and professional backgrounds, have enriched
the academic discourse by offering perspectives that are as diverse as the subject matter itself.
From theoretical explorations to empirical studies, the chapters collectively illuminate the
transformative potential of AI in shaping the entrepreneurial landscape of the future.

This book is designed to serve as a valuable resource for a wider group of readers, including
academicians, researchers, students, corporate strategists, and policymakers. By offering both
conceptual and practical insights, the present volume aims to empower readers with the
knowledge and tools needed to navigate the evolving landscape of AI-driven
entrepreneurship. As technological advancements continue to redefine the entrepreneurial
ecosystem, we hope this volume will inspire and guide readers to harness AI in achieving and
sustaining financial success and corporate sustainability.

We extend our heartfelt gratitude to all the contributors for their diligent efforts in
contributing enriched and insightful research works, without which this academic endeavour
would not be a meaningful and fruitful one. We also acknowledge the support of Bentham
Science Books, whose commitment to publishing quality academic works has enabled us to
bring this vision to fruition. It is our sincere hope that this book will spark meaningful
discussions, inspire innovative practices, and contribute to the burgeoning field of AI-driven
entrepreneurship in a meaningful way.

#### **CHAPTER 1**

### **Impact of Entrepreneurship on the Growth of** **Future India: Remodeling of Startups with** **Artificial Intelligence**

**Rupak Karmakar** <sup>**1,***</sup>

_1 Department of Commerce & Management, Barasat College, West Bengal, India_

**Abstract:** The term entrepreneur is like the creative mind that dreams big and turns
those dreams into reality. On the other hand, entrepreneurship fuels innovation and
creates structure in economic growth. In the future, innovation will be the key driver
that takes economic development to new heights. Entrepreneurship not only generates
numerous employment opportunities but also helps improve the quality of life. For a
country, entrepreneurship can be a path to economic independence. It has the potential
to lift people out of poverty and transform the nation into a developing economy.
Recently, governments and businesses have promoted startups to support economic
growth and future development. As we know, the world is changing rapidly, and the
economy is evolving alongside it. Therefore, this chapter focuses on how
entrepreneurship fosters positive change, faces challenges, and how startups are
shaping the new India with promising prospects.

**Keywords:** AI, BHASKAR, Entrepreneur, Startups.

**INTRODUCTION**

In a developing country like India, entrepreneurship is a vital parameter of
economic growth, innovation, and job creation. Due to various government
initiatives over the past decades, India’s entrepreneurial ecosystem has evolved
significantly. Entrepreneurship depends on three pillars: skill, knowledge, and
support. The entrepreneurship development programs have focused on youth, who
need good job opportunities. With the rise of the internet and the increase in
digital transactions in India, a large number of entrepreneurial activities are taking
place. One of the key factors driving entrepreneurship growth in India is the
availability of a substantial source of venture capital. During economic policy

- **Corresponding author Rupak Karmakar:** Department of Commerce & Management, Barasat College, West
[Bengal, India; E-mail: rkarmakar930@gmail.com](mailto:rkarmakar930@gmail.com)

**Narendra Kumar & Pradipta Banerjee (Eds.)**
**All rights reserved-© 2026 Bentham Science Publishers**

**2** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Rupak Karmakar_**

reforms, India’s entrepreneurship underwent significant development. The
economic policy is based on three aspects: privatization, liberalization, and
globalization. Today, startups are an important sector of entrepreneurship in India.
Startups are developing rapidly with the rise of the internet and the growth of
tech-savvy entrepreneurs. Some startups have gained global recognition, such as
‘Make My Trip’ (2000), Flipkart (2007), and Zomato (2008). The Government of
India wholeheartedly supports the startup ecosystem. In 2016, the Government
launched the Startup India initiative to support this rapidly growing industry
worldwide. A report by McKinsey & Company states that AI projects are more
likely to yield significant improvements in areas with fewer risks, helping
entrepreneurs reduce costs and enhance customer satisfaction.

**LITERATURE REVIEW**

This study examines the digital transformation of society and the growing
presence of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in the business world. It examines how
data analytics and AI have become valuable tools for startups, particularly
following the COVID-19 pandemic (Gupta & Nihal, 2021). Since the emergence
of AI, the Indian economy has changed rapidly. The article explains AI’s ability
to transform the overall economic landscape and emphasizes the necessity of
strategic adoption of AI (Swami & Kumbhar, 2022). Businesses in India have
accelerated their growth after implementing AI, and socio-economic conditions
have evolved dynamically. The chapter also highlights tactics and best practices
used by Indian business authorities (Swami _et al_ ., 2023). AI plays an integral role
in the business world and startups. In society and business, AI is becoming
increasingly important, and employees who work with AI tend to receive better
recognition. For this reason, the authors specifically highlight the impact of AI on
Micro, Small, and Medium Enterprises (MSMEs) (Yaranal & LN, 2023).

In the last couple of years, artificial intelligence has emerged as a vital part of
technology worldwide. Startup businesses are increasingly relying on AI-based
evaluations. Since the pandemic, the demand for artificial intelligence has grown
significantly compared to the previous decade. This article primarily focuses on
the development of artificial intelligence (Reddy _et al_ ., 2024). AI has had a
remarkable impact across a wide range of industries, including banking,
education, healthcare, and more. Its influence extends beyond business, affecting
the social sector as well. This article provides a brief review of AI in the business
sector (Panigrahi _et al_ . 2024).

**_Impact of Entrepreneurship_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **3**

**OBJECTIVES**

The chapter has the following objectives:

1. To examine the impact of entrepreneurship on the growth of future India.
2. To analyze the effect of startups on the Indian economy.
3. To explore the role of artificial intelligence in startups.

**METHODOLOGY**

This study focuses on two major aspects: entrepreneurship growth in India and the
effect of startups on the Indian economy. To achieve the study’s objectives, both
primary and secondary data have been used. Primary data were collected through
a structured questionnaire. We gathered responses from one hundred (100)
participants using purposive sampling and analyzed the data using statistical
software (SPSS). Secondary data were collected from websites, books, journals,
publications, and other relevant sources.

**GOVERNMENT SCHEME FOR STARTUPS**

In the twenty-first century, entrepreneurship is a transformative force for the
nation’s economy. According to the Global Entrepreneurship Monitor (GEM) and
the National Entrepreneurship Context Index (NECI), India ranks 4th out of 51
countries in terms of the quality of its entrepreneurship ecosystem. Initiatives like
the **Startup India Action Plan** provide substantial support for entrepreneurship
schemes. Startup India is a program that offers bank loans to support
entrepreneurship among scheduled castes, scheduled tribes, and women in
establishing new enterprises.

**BHASKAR:** BHASKAR is a centralized digital platform designed to bring
together startups, investors, service providers, mentors, and government bodies to
foster collaboration, knowledge sharing, and innovation across India’s rapidly
growing startup ecosystem, including Tier 2 and Tier 3 cities. The Central
Government of India categorizes cities into four major tiers: Tier 1, Tier 2, Tier 3,
and Tier 4. These classifications are based on factors, such as population size,
infrastructure development, economic growth, and standard of living.

**Key features**

a. This platform helps investors, mentors, and students register and communicate

with each other.
b. The BHASKAR scheme promotes sector-wise growth and cooperation within

the startup ecosystem.

**4** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Rupak Karmakar_**

c. Each stakeholder on the BHASKAR portal is assigned a unique ID and a

personalized dashboard, providing access to relevant tools, resources, and
collaborators.
d. BHASKAR offers startup fundamentals, business planning tools, expert

mentorship, and supports entrepreneurs from ideation to execution.

**AI Assist Tools for Startups:** The artificial intelligence-based ‘Digital India'
initiative is another bold step by the Government of India, introduced to
encourage the growth of more efficient and highly skilled MSMEs. In India,
several organizations have initiated entrepreneurship development programs,
including the Small Industry Services Institute (SISI), Industrial Development
Bank of India (IDBI), State Institute of Education Technology (SIET), Small
Industry Development Organization (SIDO), and Technical Consultancy
Organizations (TCOs).

**Miro Assist:** Miro Assist is a tool that analyzes customer feedback, expands
ideas, and provides suggestions based on that feedback. It helps startups consider
product improvements or content related to their products.

**JetBrains AI Service:** JetBrains AI tools serve as a right-hand assistant for
startup founders, helping them analyze product summaries.

**Chatspot:** From image generation to data analysis, Chatspot can handle a wide
range of activities. It is a significant AI tool transforming the startup landscape.

**AMP:** AMP designs, engineers, and manufactures robotic systems for recycling
sites. AI can quickly distinguish variations in materials, such as material type,
shape, and logos.

**TESLA:** Tesla offers several electric vehicle models and has expanded its AI and
robotics programs to experiment with bots, neural networks, and autonomy
algorithms.

**Uber:** Uber’s AI-powered mapping services are used by people worldwide to
navigate between their homes, offices, and other destinations.

Factor 1 is explained by three variables: employment opportunity (loading score =

together explain a total of 54.69% of the variance, demonstrating a good fit with
the factor analysis results.

**CONCLUSION**

In India, more than 1.4 lakh startups are currently recognized by the Department
for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade (DPIIT), but resource fragmentation
remains a challenge. Today’s era heavily relies on entrepreneurial activities, with
industries increasingly aligning themselves with AI and data analytics.
Importantly, artificial intelligence enables entrepreneurs to enhance strategic
planning by providing data-driven insights. AI is also expanding job opportunities
within entrepreneurial sectors. This technology helps entrepreneurs build
resilience and adaptability in the face of challenges. The article concludes that AIbased startups and entrepreneurial activities can enhance employment

opportunities, productivity, quality, technology, efficiency, and overall scope for
improvement. Therefore, the suitable adoption of artificial intelligence is expected
to enhance the economy’s overall system in the future.

#### **CHAPTER 2**

### **Impact of AI-enabled Social Media on Rural** **Tourism Entrepreneurship: An Empirical Study on** **Sundarbans, West Bengal**

**Dhananjoy Ghosh** <sup>**1**</sup> and **Nirmal Chandra Roy** <sup>**1,***</sup>

_1 Department of Business Administration (Human Resource), The University of Burdwan, India_

**Abstract:** In India, tourism is one of the budding sectors in terms of the economy and
employment. There are several components of the tourism sector, and rural tourism is
one of them. Rural tourism allows travellers to experience a wide range of natural and
nature-based activities, such as agriculture, fishing, trekking, and climbing. Currently,
AI-enabled social media platforms (such as YouTube, Facebook, Instagram, and
LinkedIn) play a significant role in promoting tourism businesses in rural India.
Through vlogging, blogging, and content creation on social media, a large number of
travel and tourism entrepreneurs are successfully running their business operations. In
the present chapter, we have selected the Sundarbans of West Bengal as the area of
study. The Sundarbans has limited agricultural land, and most people’s livelihoods
depend on activities, such as fishing, honey collection, and the collection of medicinal
plants, all of which fall under rural tourism activities.

This study focuses specifically on rural tourism entrepreneurs operating in the
Sundarbans who use AI-based social media as a promotional and revenue-generating
tool. Primary data were collected from 117 respondents selected through Snowball
sampling. Among them, 19 were social media tourism content creators who also
provided tourism services, while the remaining 98 were local entrepreneurs utilizing
social media for various business operations. The study further examines the income of
tourism businesses before and after integrating AI-enabled social media. After data
collection, a normality test and the Wilcoxon Signed-Rank Test were performed. The
results demonstrated that AI-enabled social media significantly contributes to the
success of rural tourism entrepreneurship. However, factors such as improved
infrastructure, favorable government policies, and security also affect income and
growth in this sector.

**Keywords:** Artificial Intelligence (AI), Entrepreneurship, Rural tourism, Social
media, Sundarbans, West Bengal.

- **Corresponding author Nirmal Chandra Roy:** Department of Business Administration (Human Resource), The
[University of Burdwan, India; E-mail: ncroy@mbahr.buruniv.ac.in](mailto:ncroy@mbahr.buruniv.ac.in)

**Narendra Kumar & Pradipta Banerjee (Eds.)**

**_Impact of AI-enabled_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **9**

**INTRODUCTION**

In this modern era, Artificial Intelligence (AI) has become a widely recognized
topic in the technological workplace. AI is gradually entering our lives through
various channels due to the easy availability of information, making our work
easier at every step. Recently, AI adoption in the digital workplace has become
common, and even social media has not remained untouched by this trend. Today,
social media is an integral part of everyone’s daily life (Kaplan, 2016). Social
media can be defined as a collection of internet-based applications built on the
ideology and technical framework of Web 2.0, which allows the creation and
exchange of user-generated content (Kaplan & Haenlein, 2009).

The 20th century witnessed a drastic change in our economic and social
environment, driven both directly and indirectly by social media (Kaplan &
Haenlein, 2009). The early 21st century marked the golden period for many major
social media platforms (Kumar & Mishra, 2015). Numerous social media
applications have gained popularity over the years, often replacing one another in
the digital communication market. Examples include Six Degrees, Myspace,
LinkedIn, Facebook, YouTube, WhatsApp, Instagram, Snapchat, and TikTok
(Boyd & Ellison, 2007).

Currently, popular social media platforms such as Facebook, Instagram,
WhatsApp, and LinkedIn process large volumes of data, identify trends and
patterns, manage user interactions, create content, and much more by integrating
AI into their systems (Jones, 2024). AI-enabled features, such as automated
responses, content creation, content tracking, voice and video calling, messaging,
content sharing, vlogging, blogging, money transactions, and post management,
have made these apps highly relevant in our daily lives (Kumar & Mishra, 2015).

As a result, entrepreneurs who adopt AI-enabled social media platforms, such as
Facebook, Instagram, Telegram, and Snapchat, benefit greatly. It has become
much easier for them to automate various operations, such as responding to
customers in their absence through pre-set chatbot responses, which aids in
managing interactions and handling queries effectively (Mohamed _et al_ ., 2024;
Brown _et al_ ., 2020).

In this way, AI is gradually establishing itself in a broader perspective,
transforming social media into an automated marketplace for various activities.
Users are increasingly exposed to new opportunities, gaining access to targeted
information, and flourishing in business through this virtual marketplace. This
level of accessibility and coverage attracts young individuals who view social
media as a means of earning a living through vlogging, page creation, product
selling, and other means, especially in a struggling job market, allowing them to

**10** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Ghosh and Roy_**

change their unemployed status in society (Marwick & Boyd, 2011; Sebastião,
2013). Consequently, a significant number of social media content creators have
emerged in recent years. Meanwhile, users enjoy endless content options on social
media tailored to their interests, all free of cost (Price, 2024; Forani, 2023). Social
media has now become a significant source of references and information, serving
as a strong guide for brand choices (Singh _et al_ ., 2021). YouTube channels and
Facebook pages act as vast information bases in various fields for viewers while
also providing income opportunities for page creators and vloggers. The
viewership of any content generates income for content creators (Meta, n.d.;
Simonson, 2024). The growing popularity of social media as an impactful
communication tool has transformed promotional trends in both the goods and
service industries (CNBC-TV18, 2022).

The Indian tourism sector is one of the oldest and fastest-growing sectors in the
Indian economy. Currently, among all tourism types, rural tourism is receiving
increasing attention, as 74% of the Indian population is part of the rural economy
and is seeking opportunities, leading to a steady migration, which is a significant
concern (Utami _et al_ ., 2023). The importance of rural tourism lies in its role in
creating employment, generating income, linking activities with value creation,
balancing wealth distribution, and exploring unused resources (Kar & Sahoo,
2016). Rural tourism is closely linked to entrepreneurial activities rooted in rural
life, generating income opportunities and driving economic development (Utami
_et al_ ., 2023). It can be described as a livelihood strategy for rural populations who
often face limited economic support (Karlsdóttir _et al_ ., 2017), helping to improve
the standard of living within local communities (Leu, 2019). However, in India,
rural tourism has yet to reach its full potential, as many people remain unaware of
local tourism destinations and their unique features. In some cases, this is due to
insufficient promotion by government and private organizations (Karlsdóttir _et al_ .,
2017).

The COVID-19 pandemic, while challenging for the tourism sector, acted as a
catalyst for the Fourth Industrial Revolution, offering new opportunities and
innovative solutions to tourism-related problems. During lockdowns and
movement restrictions, people had very limited travel options (Kumar & Reddy,
2022). Even in the post-COVID-19 period, cross-border tourism remained
restricted, resulting in a significant increase in time spent on social media due to
the increased availability of free time. Consequently, there has been a gradual
shift in consumer behavior within the tourism industry, with more people
preferring short trips or visits to nearby locations to refresh their minds and seek
enjoyment. Consequently, following the COVID-19 pandemic, most contributions
to tourism have come from rural areas (Kumar & Reddy, 2022; Christou, 2016;
Statista, 2025). Recognizing this opportunity, a group of modern entrepreneurs is

**_Impact of AI-enabled_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **11**

leveraging social media as a powerful tool for promotion and income generation.
Platforms like Facebook, YouTube, TikTok, and Instagram enable creators to earn
revenue based on video viewership. These entrepreneurs share content
showcasing their daily lives and struggles in rural areas, which appears
adventurous and intriguing to urban audiences, sparking interest in rural culture
and lifestyle. Consequently, viewership of such content has steadily grown,
encouraging more travelers to visit these regions.

This emerging trend has inspired youth in underprivileged areas, where
employment opportunities are limited, to engage in content creation, supported by
the availability of high-speed internet. It has become common for young people to
create and upload content quickly across various social media platforms
(Anderson _et al_ ., 2023).

Rural tourism vloggers and entrepreneurs have adopted a new business model that
treats social media as a multipurpose platform. They use it to showcase rural
tourism destinations, natural scenery, cultural events, and services like homestays
and local cuisine. This approach increases profitability by removing traditional
middlemen from the process.

Thus, this study aims to explore how different social media platforms influence
rural tourism entrepreneurship and business turnover through customer
relationship management, engagement, product promotion, pricing strategies, and
brand building.

**BACKGROUND OF THE STUDY**

Sundarbans is the delta land of the Ganga, Brahmaputra, and Meghna rivers,
making it the largest mangrove forest in the world (Ghosh _et al_ ., 2015;
Chowdhury _et al_ ., 2017). It covers a total area of 25,000 sq. km, out of which
9,630 sq. km falls under Indian administrative territory. It is recognized by the
UN as one of the largest biological hotspots in the world. The Indian-administered
Sundarbans has a population of approximately 4.37 million, with a population
density of 957 people per sq. km, as per the 2011 Census of India, which is the
highest among all coastal areas in India. The Sundarbans coastal region is highly
vulnerable and unstable from both climatic and economic perspectives. The
Sundarbans consists of 19 community development blocks, including
Mathurapur-1, Mathurapur-2, Joynagar-1, Joynagar-2, Haroa, Namkhana,
Canning-1, Canning-2, Sandeshkhali-1, Sandeshkhali-2, Patharpratima,
Hasnabad, Kakdwip, _etc._ (Chowdhury _et al_ ., 2017). The socio-economic status of
the people is very poor, as most depend on fishing, honey collection, wood
collection, medicinal plant collection, and even hunting, which is strictly
prohibited. Since human activity is highly restricted near the forest area, the

**12** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Ghosh and Roy_**

collection of natural resources is very limited.

Therefore, per capita income in Sundarbans is very low in all aspects. As a result,
the survival of the local people now largely depends on tourism and tourismrelated activities. According to reports, approximately 40% of the people in
Sundarbans have migrated to other states and countries for survival, which is a
major concern for the government. The Sundarbans face significant challenges,
such as limited agricultural land, lack of industries, inadequate infrastructure, and
volatile climatic conditions, making the region unsuitable for industrial
development. As a result, unemployment remains widespread.

Additionally, the approximately 2% population growth rate in these coastal blocks
further strains local resources. Rural tourism emerges as the only viable industry
capable of addressing unemployment and improving socio-economic conditions.
Despite limited government support, underdeveloped infrastructure, and minimal
coverage by traditional media, the rural tourism sector continues to grow steadily.
This growth is largely driven by the digital revolution, enhanced internet
connectivity, and the expanding influence of social media and rural tourism
content creation. Against this backdrop, the present chapter, “ **Impact of AI-**
**enabled Social Media on Rural Tourism Entrepreneurship: An Empirical**
**Study on Sundarbans, West Bengal,”** has been designed to investigate the
potential of AI-enabled tourism activities in the Sundarbans region.

**LITERATURE REVIEW**

The conceptual case study by Lupa-Wojcika (Lupa-Wojcika 2020) explored the
role of social media in entrepreneurship. The study reveals that social media is a
widely used tool among most companies, enabling them to achieve optimal
economic benefits in various ways. The study also indicates that social media is
no longer limited to communication and marketing; it is also used for online
meetings, file and document sharing, remote work support, and more (Kaplan &
Haenlein, 2009; Jones, Borgman, & Ulusoy, 2015). This multifunctional use of
social media facilitates optimal resource utilization and helps achieve an
equilibrium point in economic benefits (Pentina _et al_ ., 2012; Parveen _et al_ ., 2014).
Jangongo and Kinyua Jagongo & Kinyua (2013) described the impact of social
media on the growth of Small and Medium Enterprises (SMEs) in Nairobi. Using
cluster sampling and simple random sampling, the study selected 246 SMEs for
the final analysis. The findings showed that social media has a significant impact
on increasing market accessibility (Anand & Pande, 2024; Tajvidi & Karami,
2017). However, the customer pricing and innovativeness of social media do not
significantly contribute to the growth of small and medium-sized enterprises. The
study also reported that SMEs use social media for various purposes: 23% for

**_Impact of AI-enabled_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **13**

Customer Relationship Management (CRM), 22% for sales, 21% for accessing
new markets, and 15% for data collection. Similarly, Kowalski’s study found that
fear of negative customer feedback serves as a barrier to social media adoption in
business (Kowalski, 2020).

Oji _et al._ (Oji _et al_ ., 2017) conducted a study on SMMEs to explore their use of
social media in business. The study used a purposive sampling technique to select
SMMEs in the hospitality sector with fewer than 50 employees in Cape
Metropole. Using a quantitative approach, the study found that most restaurants
use social media platforms, with Facebook and Twitter being the most frequently
used platforms after WhatsApp. Social media platforms serve as effective
marketing tools when used for new product promotion, customer engagement, and
customer relationships (Ashley & Tuten, 2014; Brodie _et al_ ., 2013; Gummerus _et_
_al_ ., 2012). . The study also indicates that most social media platforms are
primarily used for socialization, customer engagement, and relationship building
(Brodie _et al_ ., 2011; Ashley & Tuten, 2014). Another study by Troise _et al._
(Troise _et al_ ., 2021) discussed the impact of social media on start-ups' orientations
and opportunity creation in Italy. After analysing data from 296 start-ups using
the PLS-SEM method, the study found that social media has a positive impact on
entrepreneurial opportunity (Olanrewaju _et al_ ., 2019; Nambisan, 2016). However,
it has no impact on entrepreneurial orientation programs (Fischer & Reuber, 2010;
Faiz _et al_ ., 2024). The conceptual study by Bakar and Zaini (Bakar & Zaini, 2022)
examined the impact of various factors, including performance expectancy, social
influence, facilitating conditions, information-sharing propensity, viral marketing
expectancy, and pandemic fear, on social media usage among entrepreneurs in
Malaysia. The study findings indicate that utilizing social media in
entrepreneurship enhances customer relationships and fosters a strong market
presence (Wang & Kim, 2017; Felix _et al_ ., 2016; Mandal & McQueen, 2012;
Chawla & Chodak, 2021; Tajvidi & Karami, 2017).

Velentza and Metaxas (Velentza & Metaxas, 2023) investigated the role of digital
marketing in the tourism industry by collecting 270 responses. Their study
showed that companies engaging with social media experience improved overall
performance (Tajvidi & Karami, 2017; Michaelidou _et al_ ., 2011; Luo _et al_ ., 2013;
Trainor _et al_ ., 2013; Malthouse _et al_ ., 2013). Businesses naturally prefer
Facebook over other social media platforms to build their brand image, share
information, attract customers, build trust, and establish relationships (Kumar &
Mirchandani, 2012; Ashley & Tuten, 2014; Tuten & Solomon, 2017). The study
also revealed that customer opinions are valued, as social media serves as an open
forum for dialogue, product advertisements, and information sharing. Mango and
Cassia (Magno & Cassia, 2018) explored the impact of social media bloggers on
tourism using data from 129 respondents collected _via_ bloggers. Their analysis

**14** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Ghosh and Roy_**

using covariance-based SEM showed that followers’ travel behavior is
significantly influenced by travel vloggers, with the effect depending on the
bloggers' trustworthiness and quality of information (Wang & Yan, 2022; Sparks
_et al_ ., 2013; Filieri, 2014; Jin & Phua, 2014; Cheng & Edwards, 2015). However,
this study did not separately analyze behavioral differences between loyal
followers and general social media users. Pesonen’s study (Pesonen, 2011)
examined tourism marketing practices in small and large tourism businesses in
Finland, considering 270 rural tourism SMEs, of which 40 had Facebook pages.
The findings indicated that most SMEs had recently joined Facebook, while larger
firms maintained dedicated pages. The study highlights that large firms are more
proficient and consistent in their social media use, regularly posting and creating
content, whereas small firms often engage less meaningfully with social media
(Michaelidou _et al_ ., 2011; Taiminen & Karjaluoto, 2015; Verheyden _et al_ ., 2013;
Fischer & Reuber, 2010; Guesalaga, 2015).

The study by George and N (George & N, 2019) examined the influence of social
media on tourism trends and implications in Bengaluru. Using convenience
sampling, 50 tourism service providers were selected, and the findings showed
that 66% of these providers are active on social media, making it the most
preferred promotional medium among operators (Munar & Jacobsen, 2014;
Christou, 2016; Statista, 2025b). Although social media’s influence on purchasing
decisions is consistent across different categories of tourism operators, consumer
age groups do not show significant variation in this regard (Leung _et al_ ., 2013;
Gretzel, 2006; PwC, 2019). Similarly, Țuclea _et al._ (Țuclea _et al_ ., 2020)
investigated the role of social media in enhancing health and safety awareness in
Romanian tourism. Based on a sample of 675 Romanian social media users, their
structural equation analysis demonstrated that the trustworthiness of social media
encourages tourists to seek information through these platforms. Social media
positively influences tourists’ destination choices (Yoo & Gretzel, 2010; Jeong &
Jang, 2010), as people tend to consider it more reliable than other sources when
researching travel destinations (Fotis _et al_ ., 2012; Ayeh _et al_ ., 2013; Cox _et al_ .,
2009; Yoo & Gretzel, 2010; Jeong & Jang, 2010).

Yang _et al._ (Yang _et al_ ., 2023) investigated the impact of digital interference on
the entrepreneurial behavior of rural residents in the tourism sector. Relying on
the AMO framework and SEM, the study revealed that villagers in rural tourism
destinations are positively influenced by Digital Penetration and Digital Literacy
(DPDL), Entrepreneurial Passion (EP), and Social Network Relationship (SNR),
which act as positive mediators in the relationship between Digital Penetration
(DP) and Entrepreneurial Behavior Tendency (EBT) in tourism. Kumar and
Kumar (Kumar & Kumar, 2021) also explored the role of digital media in
promoting rural tourism entrepreneurship. After using secondary data, this study

**_Impact of AI-enabled_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **15**

concluded that social media helps in content creation and user engagement
through feedback and suggestion-collecting mechanisms.

**RESEARCH GAP**

The existing literature review highlights the role of social media in
entrepreneurship and tourism, but certain gaps remain unaddressed:

Although several studies focus on SMEs and tourism, limited research specifically
investigates the impact of AI-enabled social media on rural tourism
entrepreneurship, particularly in regions such as the Sundarbans, West Bengal.
Secondly, existing studies primarily focus on traditional social media usage rather
than AI-enabled social media platforms, which could offer different dynamics in
rural tourism contexts. Thirdly, most studies are conducted in urban or
international settings, with a minimal focus on rural tourism entrepreneurship in
the Indian context, particularly in the Sundarbans region. Finally, while existing
studies have discussed the role of social media in marketing and customer
engagement, there is a lack of empirical investigation into how AI-enabled social
media influences entrepreneurial activities, such as opportunity creation,
branding, and community engagement.

**RESEARCH QUESTIONS**

Based on the identified research gaps, the following research questions are
proposed:

G
**Primary Question:**

How does AI-enabled social media impact rural tourism entrepreneurship in the
Sundarbans region of West Bengal?

G
**Sub-questions:**

How do AI-enabled social media platforms enhance entrepreneurial opportunities
in rural tourism?

How does the use of AI-enabled social media influence customer engagement,
branding, and market access in rural tourism settings?

**OBJECTIVES**

The primary objective of this study is to investigate the impact of AI-driven social
media marketing on revenue generation for rural tourism entrepreneurs.

**16** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Ghosh and Roy_**

**METHODOLOGY**

**Population of the Study**

The study focuses on rural tourism entrepreneurs using AI-enabled social media
in the Sundarbans region. However, the exact number of these entrepreneurs and
their customers (guests) is unknown, so the population size for the study cannot
be determined.

**Sample Size and Sampling Technique**

To identify tourism entrepreneurs using AI-enabled social media, snowball
sampling was applied. In this study, we identified 117 such entrepreneurs in the
Sundarbans, including content creators who also offer tourism services through
AI-enabled social media. For this research, we considered only those
entrepreneurs who have been operating their businesses for at least the past two
years with the support of AI-enabled social media.

**Data Source and Data Collection**

The study primarily relies on primary data, which was collected through

structured questionnaire. The questionnaire was designed to gather information
regarding the earnings of tourism entrepreneurs. During the data collection
a
process, tourism entrepreneurs who use AI-enabled social media were first
identified, and then the relevant information was obtained from them.

**Analytical Technique**

Based on the nature of the collected data, the Wilcoxon signed-rank test was
performed. However, before conducting this test, the normality of the data was
checked. The data analysis was carried out using SPSS software..

**DATA ANALYSIS**

To study the effect of AI-driven social media marketing on business generation
for rural tourism entrepreneurs in the Sundarbans, an effort was made to compare
their average monthly earnings before and after introducing AI in their
promotional activities on social media. The earnings data were collected directly
from the entrepreneurs. After checking the data for normality, appropriate
statistical analysis was performed.

**_Impact of AI-enabled_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **17**

**Null Hypothesis (Ho): Data is normally distributed.**

The normality test (Table **1** ) determines whether to employ parametric or nonparametric statistical methods. An approximately normal distribution of data
indicates the use of parametric tests, while a non-normal distribution suggests the
use of non-parametric tests.

**Table 1. Tests of normality.**

|-|Kolmogorov-Smirnov|Col3|Col4|Shapiro-Wilk|Col6|Col7|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|**-**|**Statistic**|**Df**|**Sig.**|**Statistic**|**Df**|**Sig.**|
|Earnings after|.149|117|.023|.930|117|.014|
|Earnings before|.143|117|.033|.953|117|.009|

**Source:** Authors’ calculation.

Table **2** presents the results of the normality test. For sample sizes below 2000,
the Shapiro-Wilk test is considered appropriate, while the Kolmogorov-Smirnov
test is recommended for larger samples. Since this study involved 117
respondents, the Shapiro-Wilk test was applied.

**Table 2. Descriptive statistics.**

|-|N|Mean|Std. Deviation|Minimum|Maximum|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|Earnings after|117|44661.1220|15691.29980|5600.00|78000.00|
|Earnings before|117|35512.5122|14687.00084|3500.00|67000.00|

The test produced p-values of 0.014 and 0.009 for the “after” and “before” data,
respectively. Both values are less than the 0.05 significance level, leading to the
rejection of the null hypothesis. Therefore, the results indicate that the data do not
follow a normal distribution.

Given the nature of the collected data, the Wilcoxon Signed-Rank Test was
deemed suitable. The normality test results precluded the use of the Paired Sample
T-test for this particular data set.

**Wilcoxon Signed-Rank Test**

**_Null Hypothesis (Ho): Earnings after and earnings before are equal._**

The descriptive statistics in Table **2** reveal variations in the mean values over time
for the same sample. The mean earnings increased from ₹35,512.51 ‘before’ to
₹44,661.12 ‘after’ the adoption of AI-enabled social media. Additionally, the

**18** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Ghosh and Roy_**

standard deviation also differs between the two periods, indicating changes in the
spread of earnings. These results suggest a positive impact of AI-enabled social
media on the earnings of rural tourism entrepreneurs, with average earnings
improving following its introduction.

Table **3** presents the results of the Wilcoxon Signed-Rank Test, which shows a Z
value with a significance (p-value) of .000, which is below the 0.05 threshold.
This indicates that the difference between the earnings before and after adopting
AI-enabled social media is statistically significant at the 5% level. This outcome
demonstrates a statistically significant difference between negative and positive
ranks.

**Table 3. Test statistics.**

|-|Earnings before - Earnings after|
|---|---|
|Z|-5.579|
|Asymp. Sig. (2-tailed)|.000|

**Source** : Authors’ calculation.

A notable distinction exists between the earnings of Rural Tourism Entrepreneurs
before and after the adoption of AI-enabled social media in promoting their
business. Hence, the stated null hypothesis is rejected at a significance level of
5%. In summary, it can be concluded that AI-enabled social media has a
significant impact on the income of rural tourism entrepreneurs in the Sundarbans
area of West Bengal, India.

**Financial Forecasting Model**

To provide a more rigorous assessment of the financial impact of AI-enabled
social media on rural tourism entrepreneurship in the Sundarbans, a
comprehensive financial forecasting model was developed (Table **4** ). This model
aims to capture the broader financial picture, considering costs, profit margins,
and potential growth rates over the next decade.

**Table 4. Financial forecasting model.**

|---|---|---|---|

|3|12,345.27|12,500|-155|

|Year|Projected Revenue Difference (INR)|Operating Costs (INR)|Net Profit (INR)|
|---|---|---|---|
|4|12,328.79|12,500|-171|

**_Model Assumptions and Parameters_**

G
Revenue Growth:

The initial revenue difference observed was approximately INR 12,370.23 with a
negative growth trend of INR -13.48 per year. However, given the emerging
digital economy and the increasing popularity of social media, it is reasonable to
assume a short-term boost in revenue before any long-term decline sets in.

G
Cost Structure:

For the model, typical operating costs include content creation, digital marketing,
internet expenses, and platform fees. We assume an average cost structure as
follows:

i. Content Creation (Video/Blog Production): INR 5,000 per month

ii. Digital Marketing (Sponsored Ads, Promotion): INR 3,000 per month

iii. Internet and Utility Costs: INR 2,000 per month

iv. Miscellaneous Costs (Equipment, Travel): INR 2,500 per month

**Total Monthly Operating Cost: INR 12,500**

G
Profit Margin:

For the purpose of this study, a conservative profit margin of 40% has been
assumed based on industry averages for small-scale tourism businesses using
digital platforms.

G
**Growth Rate:** While the model shows a decline in incremental revenue, it is

crucial to incorporate a positive annual growth factor ( _e.g_ ., 5% per annum) to

**20** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Ghosh and Roy_**

reflect potential gains from increased digital adoption, improved content quality,
and better market positioning.

**_Financial Projection (Year 1 to Year 10)_**

The model projects net profit using the following formula:

**Net Profit = (Revenue Difference - Total Costs) × Profit Margin**

Yearly revenue projections include both base revenue gains from AI and
compounding growth rates. The following table illustrates the projected net profit
over a 10-year period:

This comprehensive financial forecast provides a realistic view of potential
challenges and opportunities. While the immediate financial gains from AIenabled social media may appear modest, long-term strategic planning can unlock
substantial profitability for rural tourism entrepreneurs in the Sundarbans, helping
them transform their ventures into sustainable businesses.

**_Key Insights from the Financial Model_**

1. **Initial Revenue Advantage with Gradual Decline** : The model indicates that
while the early adoption of AI-enabled social media provides a noticeable boost
to revenue (estimated at INR 12,370.23 in the first year), this advantage
diminishes gradually due to market saturation, increased competition, and the
natural slowdown in early-stage digital benefits. This trend aligns with typical
business cycles, where initial growth tapers off as market conditions stabilize.
2. **Cost-Intensive Operations in Early Years:** With an average monthly
operating cost of INR 12,500, including content creation, digital marketing, and
platform maintenance, many entrepreneurs may face initial negative net profits.
This underscores the importance of efficient cost management and strategic
resource allocation to minimize early financial losses.
3. **Negative Net Profit in Early Years** : The first decade projects marginal or
negative net profits, highlighting the critical need for entrepreneurs to optimize
operational costs and diversify revenue streams to reach profitability. For
instance, the first year projects a net loss of INR 130, which gradually increases
to INR 333 by the 10th year, without significant cost reductions or alternative
income sources.
4. **Strategic Focus for Long-Term Profitability** : Despite the challenging early
phase, the model suggests that long-term profitability can be achieved through
targeted investments in brand building, digital skill development, and
diversified income streams, such as affiliate marketing, paid subscriptions, and
collaborations. This approach will not only stabilize cash flow but also reduce

**_Impact of AI-enabled_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **21**

dependency on a single revenue source, making the business more resilient to
market fluctuations.
5. **Importance of Digital Skills and Market Positioning** : The financial forecast
highlights the strategic importance of mastering AI-based digital tools for
content management, customer engagement, and targeted advertising to
maximize returns. Entrepreneurs who invest in these skills are likely to
outperform their peers in an increasingly competitive digital marketplace.

**CONCLUSION**

This study successfully captures the effects of AI-enabled social media on rural
tourism entrepreneurship, with the intention of providing insights into the
relationship between the use of AI-enabled social media and the income generated
from rural tourism entrepreneurship. The study shows a significantly positive
impact of AI-enabled social media on the income of rural tourism entrepreneurs.
However, this positive income effect does not directly indicate the effects of AIenabled social media support in rural tourism entrepreneurship. Several other
factors are not measured here due to time and resource constraints, such as current
infrastructure development in rural areas, various tourism-intensive policy
implementations, and recent behavioral changes among tourism customers, which
can increase the number of customers and contribute to the income growth of
rural tourism entrepreneurs. Furthermore, this chapter does not account for the
inflation rate over the years to measure the effect of income generated through
AI-enabled social media. The small sample size of the study may also contribute
to data inadequacy, making it insufficient to represent the entire population.
Considering the economic pressures faced by the rural economy and the growing
concerns about unemployment, this study serves as a pilot for a more extensive
investigation and provides valuable insights for policymakers. It underscores the
critical importance of fostering collaboration between rural tourism businesses
and AI-enabled social media platforms. Furthermore, future research can build
upon these findings by exploring the broader impacts of AI-driven social media
on customer relationship management, brand image development, and
communication strategies within rural tourism entrepreneurship.

#### **CHAPTER 3**

### **Analyzing the Impact of Artificial Intelligence on** **UPI Adoption and Start-Up Growth: A Study of** **Transaction Efficiency and Consumer Behaviour**

**Rupsa Mahapatra** <sup>**1,***</sup> **, Ajit Kumar Sabat** <sup>**2**</sup> **, Sonali Mohapatra** <sup>**3**</sup> and **Rajani**

**Abstract:** The financial industry has seen a significant transformation thanks to the
incorporation of Artificial Intelligence (AI) into Unified Payments Interface (UPI)
systems, which is especially advantageous for startups. UPI has become an even more
alluring payment platform for users as a result of AI's role in optimizing transaction
procedures, which has greatly improved transaction speed, accuracy, and system
stability. AI-driven improvements over the last ten years have shortened transaction
times and increased system uptime, resulting in a more seamless customer experience
overall. The quick rise in UPI adoption and user engagement has been directly
attributed to this efficiency, as well as AI's capacity to manage high transaction
volumes. Furthermore, AI has significantly enhanced UPI systems' capacity to detect
fraud, which has significantly decreased the number of fraud instances. Due to the
mitigation of security issues by real-time monitoring and sophisticated fraud prevention
methods, this enhancement has encouraged support towards consumer trust. Improved
security has not only given customers greater trust, but it has also enabled startups to
offer more dependable and secure services, which has further boosted UPI's user base
and market penetration. AI has sparked innovation and accelerated startup growth by
enabling financial companies to provide individualized services and maximize
operational efficiencies. Fintech companies have been able to stand out in a crowded
market by using AI-powered chatbots, predictive analytics, and automated fraud
detection. Consequently, numerous businesses have witnessed noteworthy increases in
their income and market share. AI has been essential to companies' success in the
financial environment since it helps them grow their operations and provide creative,
user-centric solutions. Due to AI's improvements to the UPI system, consumer
satisfaction has also significantly increased. Users are now more engaged and satisfied

owing to AI-enhanced features, including faster transaction processing, secure
platforms, and personalized services. Users are increasingly turning to UPI for their
digital payment needs, a shift in consumer behavior that underscores the broader trend
of AI's impact on fintech adoption and customer trust. In conclusion, by boosting
transaction efficiency, enhancing fraud detection, and encouraging startup innovation,
AI's incorporation into UPI has completely transformed digital payment systems. The
impact of AI on fintech firms is likely to grow as it develops, spurring further
innovation and supporting the ongoing evolution of the digital payment environment.

**Keywords:** Artificial intelligence, Adoption, Start-up, UPI.

**INTRODUCTION**

**Background of UPI and its Growth**

One of the innovations that has had the most significant impact on India's
financial landscape is the Unified Payments Interface (UPI), which has
revolutionized the way people and companies conduct digital transactions. UPI is
a mobile-based platform that facilitates smooth and instantaneous payments by
integrating various bank accounts. It was introduced by the National Payments
Corporation of India (NPCI) in 2016. With approximately 9.41 billion
transactions, valued at ₹15.34 trillion, processed on the platform in May 2023,
UPI has experienced exponential growth (NPCI, 2023). This enormous
transaction volume underscores UPI's pivotal role in promoting the cashless
economy envisioned by the Digital India plan (Reserve Bank of India, 2022). UPI
was created with the intention of advancing financial inclusion by providing
millions of underbanked and unbanked people in the nation with access to
banking services. UPI adoption was further accelerated by the government's 2016
demonetization push and subsequent initiatives to reduce dependency on cashbased transactions (RBI, 2017). For peer-to-peer (P2P) and peer-to-merchant
(P2M) payments, UPI is the recommended option because of its convenience and
the removal of middleman fees. The platform is a key component of India's
expanding digital economy due to its capacity to facilitate quick, secure, and
interoperable transactions.

**Role of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in UPI**

As UPI is growing rapidly, integrating Artificial Intelligence (AI) has become
essential to scaling the platform to meet the needs of an expanding user base. The
efficiency, security, and customer experience of UPI transactions have all been
improved by the use of AI technologies. Fraud detection and prevention is one of
the main areas where artificial intelligence has had a revolutionary impact.
Massive volumes of transactional data are analyzed by AI-powered systems using

**_Analyzing the Impact_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **29**

machine learning algorithms, which enable them to spot suspicious trends and
instantly flag potential fraud. As a result, UPI's security infrastructure has been
significantly enhanced, reducing the likelihood of fraudulent activity and
increasing user confidence (RBI, 2020). To ensure that payments are handled
through the most effective channels, even during times of severe network
congestion, Artificial Intelligence (AI) is also essential for transaction routing and
optimization. As a result, UPI can now process transactions more quickly and
reliably than it could with conventional payment systems (PIB, 2025). AI has also
aided in the creation of chatbots that utilize Natural Language Processing (NLP)
to assist users with transaction issues, payment status checks, and personalized
recommendations. With its ability to streamline processes and lessen the workload
for human customer support staff, these AI-powered solutions have become
essential to the user experience (McKinsey & Company, 2021a).

**Linking AI with Consumer Behavior**

It is impossible to overstate the significant impact of AI on customer behavior
within the UPI ecosystem. UPI platforms can now offer customized experiences
tailored to the interests of individual users, thanks to AI technologies. Artificial
Intelligence (AI) algorithms can enhance user engagement by offering
personalized recommendations, timely bill payment reminders, and customized
promotional offers based on user data analysis (McKinsey & Company, 2021b).
As consumers increasingly expect digital payment platforms to anticipate their
needs and provide contextually appropriate solutions, this level of personalization
has been found to boost user satisfaction and loyalty (McKinsey & Company,
2021a). Moreover, chatbots and virtual assistants have enhanced customer service
thanks to AI. These AI systems help users in real-time by guiding them through
problem-solving techniques, responding to frequently asked questions, and
guaranteeing seamless transactions. AI-powered customer care solutions have
been integrated into platforms like Paytm and PhonePe, greatly speeding up
response times and lowering the requirement for human interaction. This makes
using digital payment networks like UPI easier and more frictionless for users,
which increases their trust (RBI, 2022). Consumer adoption of UPI has been
largely influenced by the perceived convenience and security provided by AIenhanced features. Users are becoming increasingly comfortable utilizing UPI for
both small and large transactions as AI-powered fraud detection systems advance
(Kaggle, 2023). This has led to increased adoption rates, with more customers
using UPI as their primary means of making digital payments.

**30** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Mahapatra et al._**

**Impact on Startup Ecosystem**

Due to the integration of AI with UPI, India's startup ecosystem has undergone
significant growth, particularly within the financial technology (fintech) sector.
Fintech companies have successfully leveraged UPI's infrastructure to develop
innovative payment solutions that cater to a range of customer needs. AI-driven
solutions have been leveraged by startups, such as Razorpay, ZestMoney, and
CRED to enhance their service offerings. These startups have prioritized real-time
credit evaluation, optimisation of payment gateways, and user interface
enhancement (Crunchbase, 2023). AI and UPI have together proven to be to be a
game-changer for companies in terms of business scalability. With the use of AI
technologies, companies may save operating costs and increase productivity by
automating a variety of tasks like fraud detection, payment verification, and
customer service. Additionally, entrepreneurs can make data-driven decisions that
can boost customer satisfaction and spur growth with the assistance of AIpowered analytics, which offer deeper insights into consumer behavior (Sharma &
Sharma, 2025). Consequently, these firms have been instrumental in upending
established banking practices by providing customers with quicker, more
customized, and safer financial solutions. Additionally, Fintech companies can
now offer unique services, such as real-time financial advising, Buy Now, Pay
Later (BNPL) solutions, and credit scoring for underbanked populations. These
services are all built on top of UPI's core infrastructure (Asif _et al_ ., 2023). With
their accessible and reasonably priced financial services, these firms are leading
the way in financial inclusion and serving a wider demographic.

**Challenges in AI and UPI Integration**

Although the incorporation of AI into UPI has resulted in notable progress, a
number of obstacles continue to exist. Regulation compliance and data protection
are two of the most urgent issues. For AI systems to function effectively, they
require access to vast amounts of user data, which raises concerns about how this
data is collected, stored, and utilized (Kushwaha & Malpani, 2025). A few of
these problems are addressed by the Personal Data Protection Bill that the Indian
government has introduced; however, the regulatory environment is still
complicated and constantly changing. Furthermore, implementing AI technology
can be an expensive and resource-intensive process. Particularly, smaller fintech
businesses can find it difficult to fully utilize AI's potential due to its high cost
(Kushwaha & Malpani, 2025). Furthermore, obstacles may arise due to the
technical complexity of implementing AI systems, particularly for startups that
lack the necessary infrastructure or experience to oversee complex AI-driven
platforms (Oldemeyer _et al_ ., 2025).

**STATEMENT OF THE PROBLEM**

The purpose of this chapter is to examine how AI affects UPI acceptance and the
subsequent expansion of fintech companies. The study will investigate how AIdriven innovations have improved UPI's transaction efficiency, user experience,
and consumer behavior by concentrating on secondary data. Along with
discussing the opportunities and challenges that AI integration presents, the
chapter will examine the crucial role that AI plays in driving startup innovation
within the UPI ecosystem. The goal of this study is to provide readers with a
comprehensive understanding of how AI and UPI will impact India's digital
payment ecosystem in the future.

conclusions regarding consumer behavior, transaction efficiency, and the
performance of startups powered by artificial intelligence. The chapter concludes
with a summary of the key findings and recommendations for future research on
AI-driven finance solutions.

**LITERATURE REVIEW**

The vast amount of research and secondary data regarding the effects of Artificial
Intelligence (AI) on the Unified Payments Interface (UPI) and its consequences
for transaction efficiency and the expansion of fintech startups is examined in this
literature review. The assessment discusses several topics, including the
development of UPI, AI technologies, their impact on consumer behavior,

transaction processes, startup dynamics, and related challenges. It is divided into
multiple important areas as follows:

**UPI’s Evolution and Adoption**

**_Historical Background_**

In order to combine several payment methods into a single, user-friendly
platform, the National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI) introduced the
Unified Payments Interface (UPI) in 2016 (NPCI, 2023). UPI was developed to
enable instantaneous payments _via_ mobile devices and facilitate the integration of
multiple bank accounts for seamless digital transactions. This program was a
component of a larger campaign aimed at overcoming obstacles to financial
access and enhancing transaction efficiency to advance a cashless economy and
promote financial inclusion in India.

**_Metrics of Growth_**

Since its inception, UPI has grown remarkably. UPI had handled more than 9.41
billion transactions by May 2023, totaling over ₹15.34 trillion in transaction value
(NPCI, 2023). This quick growth is a result of UPI's acceptance and seamless
integration into regular banking operations. The platform is a well-liked option for
both customers and merchants because of its simplicity of use, quick processing,
and affordable transaction fees.

**_Government Initiatives_**

A major factor in the growth of UPI has been the Indian government's Digital
India program, as well as other financial inclusion initiatives. The adoption of UPI
has increased dramatically as a result of initiatives to digitize financial
transactions and educate the public about digital payment alternatives (RBI,
2022). Government incentives to embrace UPI and subsidies for digital payment
infrastructure have further expedited its expansion, encouraging a shift away from
cash-based transactions toward digital payments.

**_Analyzing the Impact_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **33**

**AI in Financial Technology (Fintech)**

**_Artificial Intelligence (AI) Technologies_**

Predictive analytics, Natural Language Processing (NLP), machine learning, and
other technologies are included in the field of AI. By improving capabilities in
areas like risk management, fraud detection, and consumer engagement, these
technologies have revolutionized the financial industry (Kaggle, 2023). Machine
learning algorithms, for example, examine vast volumes of data to identify trends
and anomalies, thereby enhancing operational effectiveness and informed
decision-making.

**_Applications of AI in Payments_**

Transaction processing, fraud protection, and customer service have all
significantly improved as a result of AI's integration with payment systems. By
immediately identifying anomalous activity and possible threats, AI-driven
systems improve transaction security (Ahmed & Pande, 2025). Furthermore, by
examining user preferences and behavior to provide customized services and
recommendations, AI provides individualized user experiences.

**AI’s Effect on the Transaction Efficiency of UPI**

**_Transaction Speed and Accuracy_**

The efficiency of UPI transactions has significantly increased with the use of AI
technologies. By streamlining routing and cutting down on errors, predictive
analytics and automated processing systems have increased transaction speed and
accuracy (Prabu _et al_ ., 2025). The implementation of AI algorithms in transaction
workflows is crucial for preserving user satisfaction and trust, as it minimizes
delays and ensures dependable performance.

**_Fraud Detection_**

AI has made major advancements in UPI platform fraud detection. Artificial
Intelligence (AI) systems employ machine learning models to continuously
monitor and analyze transactions, enabling more precise identification and
mitigation of fraudulent activity (Rani, 2024). This feature is essential for
protecting user transactions and preserving the payment system's integrity, which
bolsters users' trust in UPI's security.

**34** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Mahapatra et al._**

**_Operational Efficiency_**

By automating tedious operations and streamlining transaction processing, the
incorporation of AI has improved operational efficiency. A more effective and
error-free transaction environment is produced by AI technologies, which enable
streamlined transaction routing and decreased manual involvement (Ahmed &
Pande, 2025). This effectiveness enhances the overall functionality of UPI
systems and aids in the efficient management of large transaction volumes.

**AI’s Impact on Customer Experience and Behavior**

**_User Engagement and Personalization_**

AI-powered personalization has revolutionized how users engage with UPI
systems. AI improves user engagement and satisfaction by providing customized
recommendations and individualized notifications based on analysis of user
behavior and preferences (Rani _et al_ ., 2024). In addition to increasing user
satisfaction, personalized experiences increase adoption rates and foster enduring
commitment to UPI services.

**_Customer Service and Credibility_**

The emergence of chatbots and virtual assistants driven by artificial intelligence
has transformed customer service for UPI users. These AI solutions enhance user
satisfaction and confidence by providing prompt support and effectively resolving
issues (Kumar, 2024). Studies reveal that AI-powered customer service enhances
response times, precision, and reliability, resulting in a better user experience and
heightened confidence in UPI.

**_Adoption Rates_**

AI's beneficial effects on user support and experience have raised UPI adoption
rates. UPI has become a favored payment method due to its enhanced security
features, convenience, and tailored services, which has increased customer
adoption and usage (Razi-Ur-Rahim _et al_ ., 2024). AI’s contribution to these
improvements has been crucial in promoting the expansion and widespread
adoption of UPI.

**AI’s Impact on Fintech Startups Utilizing UPI Infrastructure for Innovation**
**and Growth**

AI has a big impact on the innovation and growth of fintech companies that use
the UPI infrastructure. AI has been utilized by startups such as Razorpay,
ZestMoney, and CRED to deliver cutting-edge financial solutions and expand

**_Analyzing the Impact_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **35**

their services (Xu _et al_ ., 2025). Features like real-time financial information,
tailored financial management, and automated credit scoring are made possible by
AI technologies, which propel startup success and disrupt the industry.

**_Operational Benefits_**

Fintech startups can reap significant operational benefits from the use of AI.
Startups can manage operations effectively and concentrate on innovation because
of AI's facilitation of process automation, cost reduction, and scalability (Sharma
& Sharma, 2025). Automating repetitive tasks and streamlining financial
processes help firms deliver cutting-edge services and stay ahead of the
competition.

**_Market Disruption_**

By providing innovative financial products and improved user experiences, AIenabled fintech businesses are upending established banking paradigms. By
utilizing UPI's infrastructure, these businesses aim to disrupt traditional banking
methods and offer more accessible and adaptable financial services (Xu _et al_ .,
2025). The financial services industry has seen major changes as a result of these
businesses' creative use of AI.

**Regulatory and Data Privacy Issues and Challenges in AI and UPI**
**Integration**

The integration of AI and UPI presents a number of regulatory and data privacy
issues. Concerns about data protection and adherence to laws, such as the
Personal Data Protection Bill, are raised by the widespread use of personal data
for AI applications (Sood _et al_ ., 2021). For UPI operators and finance firms,
addressing these privacy concerns while guaranteeing the efficient use of AI
technologies is a significant task.

**_Cost and Technical Obstacles_**

There are significant financial and technical challenges to implementing AI
technologies. For smaller companies and financial institutions, the complexity of
AI systems and the related expenses may be enormous (Modern Diplomacy,
2024). Technical obstacles can restrict the availability of AI solutions for
companies with limited resources, as they require specific skills and
infrastructure.

**36** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Mahapatra et al._**

**_Prospects for the Future_**

The integration of AI and UPI will need to adapt to new opportunities and
obstacles in the future. The development of AI technology, enhancing data
privacy safeguards, and responding to regulatory changes will be major areas of
focus (Shahu, 2025). To maximize the benefits of AI in UPI and fintech
ecosystems and overcome these obstacles, continued research and innovation are
necessary.

**_Synthesis of Literature_**

The literature study highlights the significant impact of AI on customer behavior,
the growth of fintech startups, and the efficiency of UPI transactions. AI
technologies have enhanced transaction speed, security, and user experience,
significantly improving UPI's performance. AI has also fueled innovation and
expansion in the fintech industry, helping to create new financial services and
solutions.

**RESEARCH GAP**

Despite a wealth of available data, there are still unanswered questions about how
AI will impact UPI and the broader financial ecosystem in the long run. To
address new trends, legal issues, and the broader implications of AI-driven
advancements, further research is necessary. The methodology used in this study
entails the collection and examination of pre-existing data from diverse sources in
order to tackle the study goals and inquiries.

**Research Design**

The study will employ a descriptive research strategy to systematically examine
how AI impacts the adoption of UPI and the growth of fintech startups. This
architecture is well-suited for utilizing data analysis to examine current
phenomena and understand their implications. The goal of this study is to provide
a comprehensive analysis of how AI impacts consumer behavior and UPI
transaction efficiency, as well as its role in helping startups expand.

**DATA INTERPRETATION**

**Synthesis of Results**

To provide a comprehensive understanding of AI’s impact on UPI and fintech
firms, the results of both quantitative and qualitative assessments are combined.
The synthesis includes the following:

**Comparative Analysis:** Evaluating improvements in transaction efficiency and
fraud detection by comparing pre- and post-AI adoption data (Mohan _et al_ .,
2024).

**Thematic Analysis:** Identifying key themes and trends in consumer behavior and
AI-driven startup innovations (Shahu, 2025).

**Contextual Insights:** Assessing how broader fintech trends and market dynamics
are impacted by AI-driven UPI innovations (Barroso & Laborda, 2022).

To synthesize the quantitative and qualitative analyses, we must merge
information on regulatory and privacy concerns, transaction efficiency, fraud
detection, and other metrics from both pre and post-AI implementation phases.

**_Analyzing the Impact_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **51**

This comprehensive analysis will facilitate a deeper understanding of AI’s impact
on UPI and fintech companies by comparing key parameters such as transaction
speed, fraud detection rates, and startup profitability.

**Comparative analysis: Pre and post-AI implementation metrics.**

**_Transaction Efficiency (Speed)_**

In fintech and UPI, transaction efficiency—especially speed—is highly
significant. AI has been used to optimize backend procedures, which has
improved real-time transactions (Table **11** ).

**Statistical Analysis**

**_Statistical Test for Transaction Speed Improvement_**

To evaluate the improvement in transaction speed after AI integration, a paired **t-**
**test** was applied to compare pre- and post-AI transaction times (Table **13** ).

**Table 13. Statistical test results for transaction speed improvement.**

|Metric|Pre-AI (Avg. Time)|Post-AI (Avg. Time)|
|---|---|---|
|Mean Transaction Time|2.02 seconds|1.12 seconds|
|Standard Deviation|0.21|0.31|
|t-statistic|7.34|p < 0.01|

(Sources: Compiled from collected data).

**_Analyzing the Impact_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **53**

**_Hypotheses_**

G
**_Null Hypothesis (H_** 0 **_):_** There is no significant difference in transaction speed

before and after AI implementation.

G
**_Alternative Hypothesis (H_** 1 **_):_** There is a significant improvement in transaction

speed after AI implementation.

G
**Conclusion:** The p-value is < 0.01, meaning we reject the null hypothesis. This

confirms a statistically significant improvement in transaction speed due to AI
integration.

**_Regression Analysis for Fraud Detection_**

A linear regression model was applied to measure the relationship between AI
integration and fraud detection rates (Table **14** ).

**Table 14. Regression analysis results for fraud detection.**

|Model Summary|Coefficient|Standard Error|t-statistic|p-value|
|---|---|---|---|---|
|Constant (Intercept)|66.5|2.8|23.75|0.000|
|AI Integration (Binary)|21.9|3.5|6.26|0.001|

(Sources: Compiled from collected data).

The following were the independent and dependent variables:

G
**Independent Variable:** AI integration (binary: 0 = Pre-AI, 1 = Post-AI)

G
**Dependent Variable:** Fraud detection rate.

G
**Conclusion:** According to the model, AI integration improves fraud detection

rates by approximately 21.9%. The model also demonstrates a significant
positive correlation (p < 0.01) between the two variables.

G
**AI-driven Innovations** : The use of AI to power chatbots, monitor fraud in real
