Among the financial performance indicators, revenue growth had the highest
coefficient (β = 0.41, p = 0.001), indicating that increased revenue growth is
strongly associated with AI adoption. This suggests that startups leveraging AI are
better positioned to capture market opportunities, scale their operations, and drive
significant revenue gains. Profitability (β = 0.38, p = 0.004) and cost reduction (β
= 0.37, p = 0.003) also showed strong positive associations with AI adoption,
further reinforcing the financial advantages conferred by AI integration.

The analysis revealed a positive coefficient for startup age. However, the effect
was not statistically significant (β = 0.05, p = 0.097), indicating that controlling
for firm maturity did not materially alter the main relationships between AI and
financial performance. VIF = 1.35 confirms that there is no concern about
multicollinearity.

Diagnostics performed on the regression model, including assessments of
multicollinearity, normality of residuals, and heteroscedasticity, confirmed the
validity and reliability of the results. All Variance Inflation Factor (VIF) values
were below 2, indicating no multicollinearity issues, and residual diagnostics
confirmed that the assumptions of normality and homoscedasticity were met.

The regression analysis strongly supports the hypothesis that AI adoption
positively impacts the financial performance of startups. Higher levels of AI
integration, strategic investment in AI technologies, and the perceived usefulness
of AI in decision-making are all significant predictors of improved financial
outcomes, including increased revenue growth, enhanced profitability, and
reduced costs. These findings underscore the critical role of AI adoption in
shaping the financial success of startups, providing valuable insights for
entrepreneurs, investors, and policymakers seeking to leverage AI as a strategic
asset in the competitive startup landscape.

**DISCUSSION**

This research explored the critical role of Artificial Intelligence (AI) adoption in
enhancing the financial performance of startups, particularly in Odisha. It
demonstrated that higher levels of AI integration, investment in AI technologies,
and strategic use of AI-driven decision-making significantly contribute to
increased revenue growth, profitability, and cost reductions. It was found that
startups in Odisha that have embraced AI technologies tend to outperform those
that rely on traditional business models. The findings also emphasized that the
perceived usefulness of AI in decision-making processes is the most influential
factor in achieving financial benefits. The study highlighted the importance of AI
for startups, not only as a technological tool but also as a strategic asset that can
drive financial success. For entrepreneurs, investors, and policymakers, these
findings offer valuable insights into the factors that influence successful AI
adoption and its potential to transform startup ecosystems in regions like Odisha.
Drawing parallels to Bangalore’s established ecosystem, where AI integration
scores average above 4.0 and revenue uplifts of 15–20% are reported, Odisha’s
mean integration score of 3.8 and revenue growth of 3.7 reflect a promising but
slightly lower level of maturity. Similarly, Gujarat startups benefit from stronger
government incentives and incubator networks, achieving higher profitability
margins (mean 4.0) compared to those in Odisha, which is 3.5. These
comparisons suggest that enhancing policy support, skill development, and
infrastructure—areas where Bangalore and Gujarat excel—could further
accelerate AI-driven financial success in Odisha. Tailored interventions could
bridge this gap, reinforcing Odisha’s potential to match more mature ecosystems
over the long term. The results stress the need for targeted investments in AI
technologies and skills development to ensure startups can fully leverage AI’s
potential for long-term financial sustainability and competitive advantage.
Pearson’s correlation analysis revealed that AI integration correlates with revenue
growth (r = 0.45, p < 0.01), investment in AI with profitability (r = 0.52, p <
0.01), and perceived usefulness with cost reduction (r = 0.50, p < 0.01). In the
multiple regression (Table **4** ), AI integration significantly predicts financial
performance (β = 0.32, t = 3.20, p = 0.002), as do investment in AI (β = 0.29, t =
3.22, p = 0.001) and perceived usefulness (β = 0.35, t = 3.18, p = 0.002). The
model explains 62% of the variance in financial performance (R <sup>2</sup> = 0.62, F =
24.56, p < 0.001), confirming the robustness of our findings. The results of this
study provide clear evidence supporting the hypothesis that the adoption of AI
significantly enhances the financial performance of startups. Consistent previous
research, shows a positive association between higher levels of AI integration,
strategic investments in AI technologies, and the perceived usefulness of AI in
decision-making, with key financial outcomes, such as revenue growth,
profitability, and cost reduction (Dabbous & Boustani, 2023; Aslam, Karjaluoto,

**_Evidence from Odisha_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **151**

& Varmavuo, 2021). These results underscore the transformative potential of AI
in entrepreneurial ventures, offering startups a competitive edge in dynamic
markets. AI’s ability to streamline business operations and optimize decisionmaking processes has proven to be a critical factor in driving financial success.
The correlation and regression analyses revealed strong positive relationships
between AI adoption and financial performance indicators, particularly revenue
growth and cost reduction. These findings align with existing literature,
suggesting that AI-driven startups tend to outperform their traditional counterparts
by leveraging advanced data analytics, machine learning, and automation to
enhance operational efficiency (Zhang & Kim, 2024; Aprianti _et al_ ., 2024).
Notably, the study found that the perceived usefulness of AI in decision-making
was the most influential factor, highlighting the importance of AI’s role in
providing strategic insights that guide business growth, (Sungthong _et al_ ., 2023).
Despite the clear benefits of AI adoption, this study also highlights several
challenges that startups face in fully realizing the financial gains associated with
it. High implementation costs, a lack of skilled personnel, and regulatory barriers
were identified as key constraints, which is consistent with prior research
indicating that these factors often limit the financial benefits of AI, particularly for
early-stage startups (Petković _et al_ ., 2023; Mansouri & Momtaz, 2021). To
overcome these challenges, startups need to adopt a strategic approach to AI
integration, focusing on aligning technological capabilities with business goals
and investing in AI education and training to build the necessary expertise
(Nguyen _et al_ ., 2024). Additionally, we included startup age as a control variable
to account for maturity-related effects. Although its coefficient was positive (β =
0.05), it was not statistically significant (p = 0.097). As discussed previously,
controlling for firm age did not materially alter the strength or significance of the
relationships between AI and financial performance. This confirms that our core
findings remain robust when accounting for lifecycle differences. The findings of
this study contribute to the growing body of literature on AI-driven
entrepreneurship, providing empirical evidence of the positive impact of AI
adoption on the financial performance of startups. By strategically leveraging AI
technologies, startups can achieve significant improvements in revenue growth,
profitability, and cost efficiency, positioning themselves for long-term success in
an increasingly competitive marketplace.

**CONCLUSION**

This study demonstrates the positive impact of AI adoption on the financial
performance of startups, particularly in terms of revenue growth, profitability, and
cost reduction. The findings reveal that startups with higher levels of AI
integration, greater investment in AI technologies, and a stronger perception of
AI’s usefulness in decision-making tend to outperform those with lower levels of

**152** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Das and Sahoo_**

AI adoption. These results support the notion that AI is a critical enabler of
business success in today’s competitive entrepreneurial environment. The
strategic implementation of AI technologies enables startups to optimize
operational efficiencies, enhance decision-making processes, and improve
customer engagement, which in turn drives financial success. However, the study
also highlights several challenges, such as the high costs of AI implementation
and the need for specialized skills, which can limit the financial benefits for earlystage startups. From a policy perspective, the findings suggest that governments
and industry stakeholders should consider offering targeted support to startups
seeking to adopt AI. Policies that provide financial incentives, such as grants or
tax breaks for AI investment, could help alleviate the burden of high upfront
costs. Additionally, programs aimed at improving AI education and skills
development would enable startups to build the necessary expertise to fully
leverage AI technologies. In conclusion, this study contributes to the existing
literature by providing empirical evidence of the significant financial benefits of
AI adoption for startups. As AI continues to reshape the entrepreneurial
landscape, it is imperative for startups to strategically invest in AI technologies to
remain competitive and drive long-term financial success. Finally, by
incorporating the startup age as a control variable, we demonstrate that the
positive impact of AI adoption on revenue growth, profitability, and cost
reduction remains consistent irrespective of firm maturity. While age itself did not
show a significant direct effect, its inclusion enhances the internal validity of our
model, ensuring that our conclusions reflect the true influence of AI adoption,
independent of lifecycle and experience effects. Further research should explore
the moderating factors that influence the effectiveness of AI in various industry
contexts to provide a more nuanced understanding of its broader implications.

#### **CHAPTER 9**

### **Impact of Artificial Intelligence on Human** **Resource Functions in Nepalese Organizations**

**Sumit Pradhan** <sup>**1,***</sup> **, Monika Thapa** <sup>**1**</sup> and **Radhe Shyam Pradhan** <sup>**1**</sup>

_1 Uniglobe College (Pokhara University Affiliate), Kathmandu, Nepal_

**Abstract:** This study explores the impact of artificial intelligence on recruitment and
selection processes within Nepalese organizations. Recruitment and selection are
selected as the dependent variable. The selected independent variables are machine
learning, natural language generator, automation, speech/voice recognition, expert
system, and data mining. The study is based on primary data with 103 respondents. To
achieve the purpose of the study, a structured questionnaire has been prepared. The
correlation coefficients and regression models are employed in this study. The study
found that machine learning, automation, speech/voice recognition technology, expert
systems, natural language generation, and data mining all have a positive impact on
recruitment and selection. As these technologies are more effectively utilized,
recruitment and selection processes become more efficient. The practical implication of
this study is that adopting advanced technologies can significantly enhance recruitment
and selection processes. Together, these technologies lead to more efficient, effective,
and inclusive hiring practices, thereby boosting overall organizational performance.

**Keywords:** Artificial intelligence, Human resources, Machine learning,
Recruitment, Selection.

**INTRODUCTION**

Humanoid or non-humanoid robots that behave like humans are examples of
Artificial Intelligence (AI), also known as machine intelligence. AI can be used in
enterprises to increase and improve operational efficiency. Artificial Intelligence
is a ubiquitous technology that has the capacity to yield significant financial gains
for enterprises, especially in service sectors such as banking, human resources
recruiting, healthcare, transportation, tourism, and hotel operations (Buhalis,
2006). By automating repetitive processes, AI not only increases operational

- **Corresponding author Sumit Pradhan:** Uniglobe College (Pokhara University Affiliate), Kathmandu, Nepal;
[E-mail: sumidpradhan@uniglobe.edu.np](mailto:sumidpradhan@uniglobe.edu.np)

**Narendra Kumar & Pradipta Banerjee (Eds.)**
**All rights reserved-© 2026 Bentham Science Publishers**

**156** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Pradhan et al._**

efficiency but also improves customer satisfaction (Bolton, 2018). Technology
has always been an amazing enabler, improving and empowering people,
increasing living standards, creating new opportunities, encouraging employee
innovation, and supporting an equitable workplace for all. In the contemporary
world, companies must compete with numerous companies worldwide, rather than
just a select few. The development of technologies reduces their negative
environmental effects. This illustrates how companies must continue to compete
in a rapidly evolving, innovative market.

It is becoming increasingly expected of HR directors to participate in
organizational strategy development. However, administrative duties can
frequently prevent HR professionals from playing this important role. AI has the
ability to address this issue. HR specialists often spend time on administrative
duties. This suggests a significant growth potential for AI applications (Rathi,
2018). HR departments may become more effective, and HR practitioners will
have more time to concentrate on organizational strategy planning, if more
businesses utilize AI to handle administrative tasks (Sullivan, 2019). AI software
can computerize complex processes, including compiling information from
multiple touchpoints, classifying personnel and HR issues into profiles, and
training teams—all of which are essential for HR professionals to utilize data to
its maximum potential (Jain, 2021).

Due to AI's boundless potential and uses, corporations and organizations' HR
departments have integrated AI into their operations. Businesses such as Google,
IBM, Amazon, Tesla, Apple, and others are utilizing AI in their HR processes to
creatively resolve employee-related HR issues (Aspan, 2020). Furthermore,
reluctance is also noted when it comes to the adoption and use of AI in
multinational corporations. However, companies that operate in marketing,
finance, and other areas are voluntarily using it. Notably, recent years have seen
significant advancements in the fields of pattern recognition and natural language
processing (Bhardwaj, 2020). In some data-rich environments, deep learning
using neural networks has become increasingly commonplace, demonstrating the
ability of robots to emulate adaptive human decision-making (Raub, 2018).
However, very few businesses have advanced to the big data stage of staff
management, where the potential has been clearly expressed in sufficient
judgments on a regular basis. Combining and automating safe HR transactions is
best done with state-of-the-art technology. Businesses must learn how to integrate
AI and HR transactions into their decision-making processes to remain
competitive in the contemporary global market (Brown, 2022). AI should be
trusted with administrative tasks to enhance an organization's effectiveness. The
potential for AI to transform HR and increase its relevance is something that
many HR practitioners are unable to understand (Johnson, 2020).

**_Nepalese Organizations_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **157**

Artificial Intelligence (AI) is increasingly being used in Human Resources (HR),
and in the coming years, HR professionals are expected to utilize AI more widely.
Previous studies explored how a wide range of companies that provide AI
solutions offer insightful perspectives on integrating AI into HR Johnson & Lee,
2022. The study also highlights the emergence of notable companies developing
their own AI solutions across various industries. The study highlights that,
although Artificial Intelligence (AI) has been around for some time, recent
technological developments have made it easier to use and maintain (Brown,
2022). This study focuses on human resources, although this topic area
encompasses several fascinating subfields. Meanwhile, real-world situations
necessitate numerous comprehensive human ways. However, there is no denying
that Artificial Intelligence (AI) technology can help humans accomplish a variety
of tasks more quickly and efficiently. Automation may result in the displacement
of workers as machines take over jobs that people once performed. As machines
take on intellectual jobs that were previously thought to be ordinary duties
requiring human intelligence, a growing portion of work will become more
automated (Ford, 2013). The human-computer interaction function created by AI
enhances management effectiveness, which in turn improves the functional
process for gathering, storing, and verifying the data required by an organization.
Routine tasks are being replaced by Artificial Intelligence (AI) with the least
amount of human input. AI is assisting with the employment process in several
ways, including verifying references, sending automated text messages, and
scanning resumes. According to the previous study, Artificial Intelligence has
been helping organizations make decisions, deal with uncertainty, and especially
when those decisions are ambiguous (Jarrahi, 2018). Even in this field, human
interaction is crucial. Technology cannot replace humans when subconscious
decisions are necessary to assess and expedite outcomes. A previous study
examined how Artificial Intelligence (AI) can simplify the onboarding process for
businesses and employees, as it can learn and adapt, recognizing preferences and
qualities, and make recommendations of its own (He, 2018).

As mentioned earlier, experts predict that in the next ten years, artificial
intelligence will outperform humans in services as well as in some less taxing
occupations (Grace, 2018). According to a previous study, except for an
increasing number of “gig economy workers,” artificial intelligence is expected to
put the majority of us out of business (Nuefeind, 2018). AI can effectively reduce
human subjectivity and conduct the entire hiring process in an open, transparent,
and objective manner. However, interactivity and face-to-face interactions, which
involve multiple senses, must be absent from the interviewing process. The
growing influence of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in the field of Human Resources
(HR) has previously been assessed (Nun, 2019). AI has become essential in nearly
every industry, and many HR managers aim to incorporate it into their

**158** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Pradhan et al._**

organizations, primarily to automate communication with candidates. The two key
benefits of AI in this context are the elimination of bias in candidate selection and
the enhancement of candidate efficiency. Thus, it has been determined that AI
primarily aids in the screening process by analyzing candidates' profiles,
interacting with them, and making decisions regarding the selection of suitable
candidates. Its main function is to alleviate the workload and prioritize top
candidates. Additionally, AI can perform facial recognition and conduct
interviews based on factors such as education levels and abilities.

In the context of Nepal, a previous study explored the expanding role of Artificial
Intelligence (AI) and Machine Learning (ML) in Human Resource Management
(HRM), drawing on secondary data sources such as research papers, publications,
and survey reports (Basnet, 2024). By shedding light on how AI and ML
seamlessly integrate into different facets of HR, the study emphasizes the growing
importance of this integration and explores the prospects and future trends that
these technologies bring to the forefront of human resource practices. As
organizations navigate this dynamic landscape, the adoption of AI and ML in
HRM emerges not just as a transformative trend but as a strategic imperative for
staying competitive in the evolving business ecosystem. According to the
previous study, awareness of the significance of adapting e-recruitment and
applying various management strategies has increased in private corporate
organizations (Dhakal _et al_ ., 2023). This study investigated the institutional
perception of e-recruitment adoption in the Kathmandu Valley, Nepal, by
employing the notions of the Technology Acceptance Model (TAM) and SelfDisclosure Theory (SDT). The results showed that private organizations in the
Kathmandu Valley are hesitant to fully adopt e-recruitment due to concerns about
trust and privacy issues.

The preceding discussion indicates that empirical data significantly differ among
research regarding the influence of Artificial Intelligence (AI) on recruitment and
selection procedures. Despite the aforementioned empirical evidence from other
nations, including Nepal, no current findings specifically pertain to Nepal. This
study has been undertaken to support one perspective or the other.

The major objective of this study is to examine the impact of Artificial
Intelligence (AI) on recruitment and selection processes within Nepalese
organizations. Specifically, it examines the relationship of machine learning,
natural language generation, automation, expert systems, speech/voice
recognition, and data mining with recruitment and selection in Nepalese
organizations.

**_Nepalese Organizations_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **159**

The following section describes the independent variables used in this study,
along with the hypothesis formulation:

**Machine Learning**

Earlier, the use of predictive analytics in HRM was explored, with a particular
emphasis on machine learning in the hiring process (Brown, 2019). The study
examines how machine learning algorithms can enhance the accuracy of candidate
assessments, thereby facilitating more data-driven and informed recruiting
decisions. A previous study examined how hiring procedures can be improved by
optimizing candidate selection from a machine learning perspective (Tian _et al_ .,
2020). The study examined the potential of machine learning algorithms to
evaluate various datasets and predict the eligibility of candidates, providing
valuable insights to enhance the effectiveness of the selection process. To
evaluate the effects of different machine learning algorithms on candidate
screening in the field of human resources management, a comparative analysis of
these algorithms was previously conducted (Garcia, 2020). When implementing
machine learning technologies in their recruiting and selection processes, firms
can make well-informed decisions thanks to the study's insights regarding the
efficacy of various AI-driven methodologies. Based on it, this study develops the
following hypothesis:

H1: There is a positive relationship between machine learning and recruitment and
selection.

**Natural Language Generator**

A previous study discussed how to understand natural language in the same way
people do (Matsuo, 2016). Natural language processing utilizes artificial
intelligence to process real-world data, interpret it, and make sense of it in a
manner that a computer can comprehend, whether the language is spoken or
written. Computers use programs to read and microphones to gather audio, much
like humans use their senses—ears to hear and eyes to see. Similarly, just as the
human brain processes information, computers rely on software to perform the
same function. The input is translated to code that the computer can interpret at
some point during the processing. A previous study examined how natural
language creation is used in predictive analytics to evaluate candidates (Kim,
2021). The study demonstrates how data-driven methods, such as natural
language generation, for the selection process lead to more thorough and accurate
candidate assessments. This study formulates the subsequent hypothesis:

**160** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Pradhan et al._**

H2: There is a positive relationship between natural language generation and
recruitment and selection.

**Automation**

Previous studies have concentrated on the use of automation in interview
scheduling, emphasizing how it can maximize hiring process efficiency (Kim,
2021). Automated scheduling systems can significantly reduce coordination work,
freeing up recruiters' time for applicant engagement and assessment. A
comparative analysis of business practices investigated the strategic
implementation of automation in recruitment, offering insights into effective
approaches by exploring how organizations across various industries apply
automation tools to streamline different stages of recruitment and selection (Ore
and Sposato, 2022). Another study, as described earlier, examined best practices
at the intersection of diversity and automation in hiring (Brown and Garcia,
2022). The findings suggest that automated processes can enhance hiring
outcomes by promoting fairness, mitigating bias, and fostering diversity in
candidate pools. These findings form the basis for the following hypothesis.

H3: There is a positive relationship between automation and recruitment and
selection.

**Speech Recognition**

A previous study investigated the accuracy and fairness of speech recognition
technologies in candidate evaluations through a comparative analysis (Brown &
Garcia, 2022). This study evaluated the extent to which voice recognition
software contributes to objective assessments, particularly in situations where
effective verbal communication is crucial for employment success. Previously, the
technology has been emphasized for its ability to increase productivity and
pinpoint essential skills (Kim & Johnson, 2022). The study concluded that voice
recognition software can help recruiters by streamlining the processing of
candidate responses, freeing them up to concentrate on more advanced tests
during the hiring process. This study formulates the subsequent hypothesis:

H4: There is a positive relationship between speech/voice recognition and
recruitment and selection.

**Expert System**

The function of expert systems in candidate communication was previously
examined (Patel & Wang, 2020), which emphasized how these systems enhance

**_Nepalese Organizations_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **161**

communication and the overall candidate experience. The study demonstrated that
expert systems can be utilized to automate communication procedures, leading to
prompt and customized interactions at every stage of the hiring process. Earlier, a
comparative review was conducted of the efficacy of expert systems in major
organizations, with a focus on their application in optimizing recruitment
expenses (Garcia & Kim, 2021). The results demonstrated the beneficial effects of
expert systems on decreasing turnover rates, shortening the time it takes to fill
positions, and raising the overall cost-effectiveness of the hiring process. This
study formulates the subsequent hypothesis:

H5: There is a positive relationship between expert systems and recruitment and
selection.

**Data mining**

Data mining techniques can significantly enhance the recruitment and selection
process. Data mining helps identify potential candidates by analyzing large
datasets, including resumes, social media profiles, and professional networks.
This enables recruiters to source candidates who match the job requirements more
accurately (Chien and Chen, 2008). Data mining enhances the recruitment and
selection process by improving candidate sourcing, decision-making, predictive
analytics, reducing bias, and increasing efficiency, all of which contribute to
better hiring outcomes (Shehu and Saeed, 2016). Automating the recruitment
process through data mining reduces the time and cost associated with manually
reviewing large volumes of applications. This increased efficiency enables
recruiters to concentrate on higher-value tasks, such as interviewing and engaging
with candidates (Algur _et al_ ., 2016). Based on these insights, the following
hypothesis is proposed:

H6: There is a positive relationship between data mining and recruitment and
selection.

The remainder of this study is organized as follows: Section two describes the
sample, data, and methodology. Section three presents the empirical results, and
the final section provides a conclusion.

**METHODS**

The study used a descriptive and causal-comparative approach to investigate the
influence of Artificial Intelligence (AI) on Human Resource (HR) activities inside
Nepalese enterprises. Primary data were obtained via a standardized questionnaire
administered to 103 individuals employed in HR or managerial positions. A

**162** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Pradhan et al._**

convenience sample strategy was employed to select participants based on their
availability and readiness to engage. The questionnaire comprised sections on
demographic information and AI applications in HR operations, including
recruiting and selection, machine learning, natural language processing,
automation, speech recognition, expert systems, and data mining. Responses were
documented using a 5-point Likert scale, spanning from “strongly disagree” to
“strongly agree.” The questionnaire was pre-tested with 10 respondents to ensure
clarity and relevance, and reliability was verified using Cronbach’s alpha, which
surpassed 0.70. Data were analyzed using descriptive statistics to capture
perceptions, alongside causal-comparative methodologies such as t-tests and
ANOVA to identify significant changes contingent upon organizational and
responder characteristics. Ethical considerations were meticulously adhered to
during the research process. Informed consent was obtained from all participants,
who were assured that their responses would be kept anonymous and used
exclusively for academic purposes. The study adhered to institutional ethical
norms and did not involve any interventions or sensitive personal data that
necessitated formal ethical board approval.

**The Model**

The model used in this study assumes that recruitment and selection depend upon
various artificial intelligence factors. The dependent variable selected for the
study is recruitment and selection. Similarly, the selected independent variables
are machine learning, natural language generator, automation, speech recognition,
expert system, and data mining. Therefore, the model takes the following form.

RS = β0 + β1 ML + β2 NLG+ β3 AT + β4 ES+ β5 SR + β6 DM + e

Where,

RS= Recruitment and Selection

ML= Machine Learning

NLG= Natural Language Generator

AT= Automation

SR= Speech Recognition

ES= Expert System

DM= Data Mining

**_Nepalese Organizations_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **163**

Machine Learning was evaluated through items examining its perceived
effectiveness in enhancing recruitment and selection processes. A sample item
includes, “Machine learning significantly helps in recruitment and selection in
Nepalese organizations.” The scale demonstrated acceptable reliability (α =
0.749).

Natural Language Generation was assessed through items related to the clarity
and quality of job descriptions and recruitment communication produced _via_ AI
tools. For instance, one item states, “Natural language generation technology can
effectively create compelling job descriptions and other recruitment materials.”
The reliability coefficient was α = 0.753.

Automation captured perceptions of how AI automates repetitive HR tasks.
Sample items include statements such as, “Automation can streamline repetitive
tasks in the recruitment and selection process, allowing HR professionals to focus
on more strategic activities.” The reliability of the automation scale was high (α =
0.874).

Speech Recognition was measured through items assessing its role in transcribing
and analyzing interview responses. A representative item is, “Speech recognition
technology can enhance the efficiency and accuracy of candidate interview
evaluations.” This scale had a reliability score of α = 0.742.

Expert Systems were examined through items exploring their contribution to
standardized and consistent candidate evaluations. A sample item reads, “Expert
systems can effectively assist in the evaluation of candidate qualifications during
the recruitment and selection process.” The reliability coefficient was α = 0.862.

Data mining items focused on AI’s capacity to analyze candidate data for pattern
recognition and improved decision-making. One such item states, “Data mining
techniques can help identify hidden patterns and trends in candidate data.” The
internal consistency was α = 0.834.

Finally, Recruitment and selection effectiveness were measured to assess how AI
technologies have influenced hiring decisions and process efficiency. A sample
item includes, “The implementation of artificial intelligence technologies affected
the efficiency of my organization's recruitment and selection process.” The scale
yielded a Cronbach’s alpha of 0.765.

**164** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Pradhan et al._**

**RESULTS AND DISCUSSION**

**Correlation Analysis**

An analysis of the data was conducted, and correlation analysis was undertaken
first. For this purpose, Kendall’s Tau correlation coefficients, along with means
and standard deviations, were computed, and the results are presented in Table **1** .

This table presents Kendall’s Tau coefficients between dependent and
independent variables. The correlation coefficients are based on 103 observations.
The dependent variable is RS (Recruitment and selection). The independent
variables are ML (Machine Learning), AT (Automation), SR (Speech
Recognition), ES (Expert System), NLG (Natural Language Generator), and DM
(Data Mining).

**Table 1. Kendall’s tau correlation coefficients matrix.**

|Variables|Mean|SD|RS|ML|AT|SR|ES|NGL|DM|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|**RS**|4.345|0.587|1|||||||
|**ML**|3.979|0.585|0.484**|1||||||
|**AT**|4.200|0.557|0.538**|0.508**|1|||||
|**SR**|4.243|0.522|0.461**|0.425**|0.519**|1||||
|**ES**|4.224|0.505|0.635**|0.508**|0.486**|0.613**|1|||
|**NLG**|4.228|0.537|0.658**|0.491**|0.574**|0.539**|0.614**|1||
|**DM**|4.139|0.639|0.663**|0.505**|0.615**|0.444**|0.575**|0.557**|1|

Source: Compiled from Collected data using SPSS Notes: The asterisk signs (**) and (*) indicate that the
results are significant at one percent and five percent levels, respectively.

Table **1** shows a positive correlation between machine learning and the
effectiveness of the recruitment and selection process. It suggests that greater
utilization of machine learning leads to improved recruitment and selection
outcomes. Similarly, the finding shows a positive correlation between automation
and recruiting and selection. It suggests that an increase in automation leads to
better recruitment and selection. Furthermore, there is a positive correlation
between the use of speech/voice recognition technology and recruitment and
selection. This means that an increase in the usage of speech/voice recognition
technology results in an increase in recruitment and selection. Moreover, there is a
positive relationship between the identification of expert systems and the
processes of recruiting and selection. It suggests that the expansion of expert
system capabilities leads to a more effective recruiting and selection process.
Similarly, natural language generation also has a positive correlation with

**_Nepalese Organizations_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **165**

recruitment and selection. This suggests that translating diverse languages leads to
an increase in recruitment and selection. Furthermore, there is a positive
correlation between data mining and recruiting and selection. It demonstrates that
improved data mining leads to a more effective recruitment and selection process.

Table **2** shows that the beta coefficients for machine learning are positive for
recruitment and selection. It indicates that machine learning has a positive impact
on recruitment and selection in Nepalese organizations. This result aligns with the
findings of Tian _et al_ . (2020). Similarly, the beta coefficients for automation are
also positive, indicating that automation has a positive influence on the
recruitment and selection process. This finding is consistent with the results of
Ore and Sposato (2022). Similarly, the beta coefficients for speech recognition are
positive, indicating a favorable impact on recruitment and selection. This finding
aligns with the results of Brown and Garcia (2021). The beta coefficients for
expert systems also show a positive relationship, suggesting their beneficial role
in recruitment and selection, consistent with prior findings of Garcia and Kim
(2021). Likewise, natural language generation demonstrates a positive impact,
supporting earlier research of Kim (2021). Finally, the positive beta coefficients
for data mining indicate its effectiveness in enhancing recruitment and selection,
in line with the findings of Chien and Chen (2008).

**CONCLUSION**

The study revealed that technological advancements, including machine learning,
natural language generation, automation, expert systems, speech/voice
recognition, and data mining, significantly enhance the recruitment and selection
processes within Nepalese organizations. These technologies contribute to more
efficient, accurate, and data-driven hiring practices, ultimately improving
organizational effectiveness in talent acquisition. As Nepalese companies
increasingly adopt these innovations, they are likely to experience a competitive
edge in attracting and selecting the best candidates, aligning their recruitment
strategies with global best practices. The study also concluded that data mining is
the most influential factor in determining the recruitment and selection processes
within Nepalese organizations.

#### **CHAPTER 10**

### **Exploring AI's Role in Startup Success: A** **Framework for Future Entrepreneurs**

**Sanjeeb Kumar Dey** <sup>**1,***</sup> and **Debabrata Sharma** <sup>**2**</sup>

_1 Department of Commerce, Ravenshaw University, Odisha, India_

_2_ _Department of Finance, Biju,Patnaik Institute of Information Technology and Management_
_Studies, Odisha, India_

**Abstract:** This study systematically examines AI as a significant enabler in
entrepreneurship, focusing on its potential impact on opportunity creation, decisionmaking, financial exploration, performance improvement, and education. The objective
of this study is to explore stakeholder perceptions regarding the role of AI in startups,
specifically examining how AI can assist in entrepreneurial processes, including
financial aspects. A survey will be conducted using Likert scale statements to garner
information on perceptions of artificial intelligence in the context of entrepreneurship.
The data collected was analysed using the Structural Equation Modeling (SEM)
technique in SmartPLS to test the model of the “AI-enabled entrepreneurial process.”
The study reveals how AI is perceived as an enabler in various entrepreneurial
contexts, including its implications for opportunity generation, innovative financing,
and performance improvement. The analysis evaluates the validity of the suggested
framework. The study also provides insights into effectively integrating AI technology
to optimize decision-making and improve business performance. Furthermore, the
findings will help build focused AI-related content into entrepreneurial curricula,
ensuring that future entrepreneurs possess the requisite skills to utilize AI.

**Keywords:** Artificial intelligence, Decision making, Entrepreneurship, Financial
opportunity, Start-ups.

**INTRODUCTION**

Entrepreneurship plays a vital role in economic development by channeling scarce
resources for maximum utilization, thereby benefiting society at large. It is a
means for employment generation, promoting innovation, fostering competition,
and accelerating growth. The importance of entrepreneurs can never be overstated
in any country, regardless of its level of development. Over the years, the
dimensions of entrepreneurship have drastically changed to cope with the evol

- **Corresponding author Sanjeeb Kumar Dey:** Department of Commerce, Ravenshaw University, Odisha, India;
[E-mail: kumarsanjeebdey@gmail.com](mailto:kumarsanjeebdey@gmail.com)

**Narendra Kumar & Pradipta Banerjee (Eds.)**
**All rights reserved-© 2026 Bentham Science Publishers**

**170** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Dey and Sharma_**

ving needs of consumers, technological advancements, market competition,
complex business structures, and modes of operation, among others. Among all
other factors, technology has the most significant influence on entrepreneurship.
The evolution of digital platforms, e-commerce, virtual markets, social networks,
_etc._, has removed the barriers of entry for new businesses from anywhere in the
world. Technology became the catalyst for business after the Industrial
Revolution. However, it turns out to be a conundrum for future entrepreneurs.

In nurturing a vibrant entrepreneurial ecosystem, greater momentum has been
gained by entrepreneurial training and development over the past two decades.
The rising focus on entrepreneurship education and training weakens the
conventional notion that ‘entrepreneurs are born, not made.’ To seize emerging
opportunities and drive economic growth, entrepreneurial education empowers
not only aspiring individuals but also established business practitioners. Research
consistently demonstrates a positive correlation between entrepreneurial learning
and active business engagement. Over time, Indian society’s earlier belief that
business is a perilous and unorthodox career path has gradually shifted. To fuel
this transformation, both government and private bodies offer various
incentives—ranging from financial backing and mentoring schemes to incubation
hubs—to encourage and enable individuals to step into entrepreneurship. This
growing institutional support system lays the groundwork for a new generation of
informed, confident, and resilient entrepreneurs.

Artificial Intelligence stands out as a cutting-edge and transformative
technological force with the capacity to redefine the landscape of entrepreneurial
growth and national prosperity, especially in countries like India. From
opportunity recognition and task automation to market outreach, operational
refinement, and accessing unexplored funding avenues, AI proves to be a
remarkable enabler. It empowers entrepreneurs to make smarter, data-driven
choices while reducing manual load. Research findings confirm that startups
leveraging AI tend to outperform conventional enterprises in terms of
performance and scalability, reinforcing AI's pivotal role in the future of
enterprise.

**LITERATURE REVIEW AND HYPOTHESIS DEVELOPMENT**

The integration of AI into the entrepreneurial landscape indicates a transformative
shift (Robledo _et al_ ., 2023). Through innovation, entrepreneurs create value as AI
reshapes strategy, decisions, and the way businesses operate. As AI transcends
simple automation, entrepreneurial ventures are conceptualized, strategized, and
operationalized differently (Lévesque _et al_ ., 2022). New opportunities and
business models are being reimagined as AI improves not only efficiency but also

**_Future Entrepreneurs_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **171**

overall innovation (Nambisan _et al_ ., 2019). AI reveals hidden insights and plays a
crucial role in identifying new opportunities. With tools like predictive analytics,
entrepreneurs can act on recent market trends and tap into new opportunities
(Chalmers _et al_ ., 2021). Idea creation, product design, and market fit are improved
as tools like ChatGPT and generative AI give creative answers and real-time
feedback (Vecchiarini & Somià, 2023). AI offers cost-effective ways to assess
feasibility and market potential, improving entrepreneurs' capacity to test new
ideas rapidly (Bell & Bell, 2023). With only a few studies available, it's essential
to explore how stakeholders, such as entrepreneurs and investors, perceive AI’s
role in identifying new opportunities. As the use of AI grows, businesses shift
from traditional methods to smart, flexible, data-based ways of working
(Nambisan _et al_ ., 2019).

Educational innovation is accelerated as AI provides adaptive learning systems,
intelligent tutoring, and real-time feedback (Zawacki-Richter _et al_ ., 2019). AI also
offers personalized support and automated prototyping, enhancing students'
learning experiences, particularly in entrepreneurship education (McGrath _et al_ .,
2023). Entrepreneurship education must incorporate cutting-edge technologies to
prepare students for the evolving business landscape (Zhou _et al_ ., 2024).
However, despite these advancements, challenges such as ethical concerns and
educators' skepticism still persist (Zhou _et al_ ., 2024). As AI advances alongside
entrepreneurship education, doubts persist about how to effectively utilize it in
teaching to develop skills, foster innovation, and support research (Bell & Bell,
2023). A previous study investigated how AI and IoT can enhance entrepreneurial
creativity and innovation (Hisrich & Soltanifar, 2021), finding that these
technologies offer benefits such as promoting individual and team-based
innovation, streamlining operations, and enabling businesses to stay competitive
in dynamic markets. A study revealed how a digital shift is shaped by an
entrepreneurial mindset at the Estonian Business School and found that fostering
innovative mindsets and digital competencies can help firms utilize technology
more effectively and navigate future changes (Kooskora, 2021).

AI has significant potential to revolutionize financial management. From securing
funding to automating key financial processes, AI tools have enhanced the ability
of startups to sustain their operations and achieve rapid growth (Giuggioli &
Pellegrini, 2023). Entrepreneurs make data-driven decisions that enhance
financial performance and sustainability by utilizing AI-based predictive financial
tools (Chalmers _et al_ ., 2021). AI can analyse market conditions in real-time,
thereby helping entrepreneurs attract investors and secure funding (Giuggioli &
Pellegrini, 2023). How new technologies, such as AI, blockchain, and cloud
systems, are transforming startup finance by opening doors beyond traditional
funding methods was the aim of the research conducted by (Lynn & Rosati,

**172** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Dey and Sharma_**

2021). They found that entrepreneurial ecosystems are being reshaped as funding
options, such as crowdfunding and token sales, give founders more freedom,
wider investor reach, and new ways to raise capital. AI facilitates operational
efficiency through resource allocation optimization, task automation, and
enhanced decision-making processes (Lévesque _et al_ ., 2022). Using AI-powered
tools, entrepreneurs manage workflows more effectively and scale their
businesses to meet market demands (Zhou _et al_ ., 2024). By streamlining
marketing, sales, and customer engagement, AI helps accelerate growth and
enhance business performance (Chalmers _et al_ ., 2021).

The incorporation of Artificial Intelligence (AI) into corporate operations
enhances decision-making skills and provides entrepreneurs with access to realtime analytics and predictive insights (Nambisan _et al_ ., 2019). AI tools provide
more precise forecasts of consumer preferences and market trends, facilitating
informed strategic decision-making and reducing uncertainty (Zhou _et al_ ., 2024).
Pricing plans, entering new markets, and product development are crucial
decisions that are influenced by the information provided by these technologies
(Giuggioli & Pellegrini, 2023). Entrepreneurs are depending more and more on
AI-powered decision support systems to automate crucial processes like risk
management, resource allocation, and opportunity recognition (Battisti _et al_ .,
2022). Entrepreneurs equipped with data-driven insights from these platforms are
better able to make informed decisions. AI integration is reshaping both local and
global strategies, enabling entrepreneurs to adjust to changing market situations.
Entrepreneurs enabled by AI tools focus on higher-level tasks that require creative
thinking and strategic planning, as monotonous processes are automated (Garbuio
& Lin, 2021). AI is a catalyst for long-term entrepreneurial success, enhancing
operational effectiveness, bolstering market competitiveness, and promoting
sustainable growth (Chalmers _et al_ ., 2021). When entrepreneurs use AI tools,
business objectives are met more quickly and businesses are better positioned in
competitive markets (Giuggioli & Pellegrini, 2023). However, algorithmic bias
and technological dependence are certain concerns related to AI that should be
carefully considered (Lévesque _et al_ ., 2022).

Although interest in the role of AI and entrepreneurship is increasing, many areas
still remain unaddressed. While existing studies highlight AI’s role in improving
performance, decision-making, and financial results, there is still limited evidence
on how various stakeholders view its influence. Even as AI becomes increasingly
embedded in business practices and entrepreneurial education, the viewpoints of
stakeholders across various roles are not well-documented. This study intends to
bridge these gaps by examining how AI affects entrepreneurial success, financial
management, and opportunity creation. The primary objective of this study is to

**_Future Entrepreneurs_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **173**

understand stakeholder perceptions of AI’s role in startup processes, particularly
concerning financial dimensions and overall entrepreneurial efforts.

To achieve our objectives and based on the literature, we have formulated five
areas related to entrepreneurial/ startup success that AI has influenced:

G
AI-enabled opportunity creation

G
Accelerators in education and research

G
Improved financial outcomes

G
Performance improvement

G
Enhanced decision-making

**METHODOLOGY**

This study uses quantitative methods of descriptive cum exploratory research
design to study the causal concepts and relationship between exogenous and
endogenous variables. The study employs a survey technique, where primary data
were collected using a structured questionnaire with each construct comprising 4
statements. All the variables considered for this study are extracted and adapted
from the existing literature (Table **1** ).

**Table 1. Variable details.**

|Type of Variable|Variable Name|Source|
|---|---|---|
|Dependent Variables|Opportunity Creation|Giuggioli and Pellegrini (2023)|
|Dependent Variables|Accelerators in Education and Research|Accelerators in Education and Research|
|Dependent Variables|Performance Improvement|Performance Improvement|
|Dependent Variables|Decisions making|Decisions making|
|Dependent Variables|Financial Outcomes|Lynn and Rosati (2021)|
|Independent Variable|Entrepreneurial Success|Giuggioli and Pellegrini (2023)|

Source: Authors' compilation

The variable “Opportunity Creation” focuses on how AI aids in market discovery,
trend analysis, product innovation, and adaptive business model transformation,
thereby facilitating new entrepreneurial avenues. “Accelerator in Education and
Research” demonstrates how AI enhances continuous learning, market research
quality, and innovation through real-time insights and strategic discovery
platforms. The construct “Financial Outcomes” represents how AI amplifies
financial performance, facilitates the acquisition of funding, enhances
sustainability, drives rapid business growth, and yields returns through automation
and data-driven insights. The role of AI in enhancing operational efficiency,

**174** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Dey and Sharma_**

boosting the scalability of startups, optimizing resource utilization, and
strengthening marketing and sales is reflected in “Performance Improvement”.
The support of AI in reducing strategic uncertainty, revealing customer insights,
and supporting timely and data-driven decisions, as well as enhancing accuracy in
pricing, market entry, and product strategies, is captured by the dependent
variable “Decision-Making”. The independent variable used in the study, _i.e._,
“Entrepreneurial Success,” reflects how AI enhances competitiveness, improves
business processes, accelerates the achievement of goals, and increases the
likelihood of long-term success for startups.

**Fig. (1).** Conceptual framework.

Purposive and Convenience sampling methods were used to identify and collect a
total of 187 respondents in September and October 2024. Out of which 166
complete responses are considered for analysis and discuss. Since the subject
matter involves technical aspects, it is very difficult to trace enough respondents.
However, a sample size of 100 to 200 is usually a good starting point for
conducting path modeling (Hoyle, 1995); thus, the sample used in this study is
sufficient for analysis. The respondents primarily comprise four stakeholders:
entrepreneurs, Management Students, Trainers and Instructors, and
Bankers/financiers. The measurement scale for data collection uses a 5-point
Likert scale, ranking from Strongly Disagree (1) to Strongly Agree (5),

**_Future Entrepreneurs_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **175**

respectively. The collected data was analysed through partial least squares
Structural Equation Modelling with the Smart PLS software to test the model of
the “AI-enabled entrepreneurial success. The conceptual framework developed for
this study is presented in Fig. ( **1** ), which illustrates the AI enablers across five
dimensions of entrepreneurs/startups, and their impact on entrepreneurial success.

**RESULTS AND DISCUSSION**

**Demographic Characteristics**

The demographic characteristics of the respondents are presented in Table **2** . The
sample consists of 166 participants, comprising 57% males and 43% females.
Most respondents fall within the 31-40 age group (42%), followed by the 20-30
and 41-60 age groups, both at 24%. A smaller portion (10%) belongs to the 61
and above category, indicating diverse age representation. The roles of the
respondents reveal that the smallest group comprises entrepreneurs (17%),
followed by bankers or financiers (20%), trainers or instructors (28%), and the
largest group is MBA students (35%). In terms of experience, 18% of respondents
are early-career professionals with 0-1 year of experience, 36% have more than 5
years of experience, and 46% have 1-5 years of experience. Regarding AI
awareness, the smallest group (18%) consists of beginners, followed by 39% of
advanced users, and the majority (43%) consider themselves to be at an
intermediate level. Participants are engaged across various AI application areas,
with the majority (33%) involved in all areas (idea development, finance, and
marketing). The remaining respondents are distributed across idea development
(21%), marketing (22%), and finance arrangement (24%).

**Assessment of Measurement Model**

Harman’s single-factor test was conducted to assess the potential presence of
common method bias. A single factor solution explained 31.699% of the total
variance, which is significantly below the 50% benchmark commonly used as a
threshold (Latif _et al_ ., 2020). This result suggests that common method bias does
not pose a significant concern in this study, and the data can be considered
reliable for further analysis.

Model validation for outer measurements has been performed by examining the
construct validity and discriminant validity. The former is tested using Average
Variance Extracted (AVE) for each latent variable (Table **4** ). At the same time,
the latter is evaluated through the Fornell-Larcker Criterion test and the HTMT
test (Tables **6** and **7** ). Similarly, for evaluating inner model validation, we have
applied structural path significance in Bootstrapping using a two-tailed t-test with
a 5% level of significance (Table **7** ).

Table **5** show the validity and reliability of each indicator for structural model
formulation and accuracy in obtaining hypothesis testing results. The terms
reliability and validity refer to obtaining accurate and consistent results with a
threshold limit for model specification and validation of quantitative data. The
reliability was measured through Cronbach’s alpha, and convergent validity was
assessed through factor loading, composite reliability, and AVE score (Hair _et_
_al_ .,2017). Table **3** shows that all factor loadings are above the threshold limit of
0.6 (Henseler, 2017), thus indicating good convergent validity. Based on the
factor loading score, OC1, PI1, and ES1 have been dropped from further analysis.
In addition, Table **4** shows that the Cronbach's alpha and composite reliability of
all constructs are greater than 0.7. The average variation of individual constructs
is greater than the three-hold limit of 0.5 (Nuseir _et al_ ., 2020), which indicates
that all constructs have fulfilled their reliability and validity measurement limits
for accurate model construction and optimal authentication results. The VIF value
of all the statements is less than the threshold limit, which is ‘5’ (Hair _et al_ .,
2011). This indicates that there is no potential issue with collinearity among the
constructs. That means all the observations are independent and there is low
correlation between the predictors and other predictors, allowing for rational
decisions with no multicollinearity issues.

Tables **6** and **7** describe the discriminant validity through the heterotrait-monotrait
(HTMT) ratio and Fornell-Larcker criterion analysis. The discriminant validity
describes the extent to which the items differentiate the constructs and measure
different concepts, meaning we have compared the average variance within the
construct with the variance of another construct in a matching row and column.
The Fornell-Larcker criterion shows that the square root of the AVE value within
the same construct in the diagonal elements (0.811, 0.790, 0.831, 0.758, 0.802,
and 0.832) is higher than the relationship with other constructs, and the HTMT
matrix also fulfills its threshold limit, which is less than the 0.85 cutoff value. In
other words, discriminant validity is also measured through cross-loading, which
means that the loading factor of the corresponding constructs is higher than that of
all other constructs. Hence, both Tables **5** and **6** fulfil the criteria and confirm the
discriminant validity for further processing and analysis.

**Structural Model Assessment**

After measuring the validity and reliability of the construct, we formulated the
measurement model using the PLS-SEM algorithm and bootstrapping, with a
sample size of 5000 units for hypothesis testing and obtaining path coefficient
results. The formulated model, as shown in Fig. ( **2** ), indicates that AI exploration,
encompassing opportunity creation, education and research, financial outcomes,
decision-making, and performance improvement, significantly influences
entrepreneurial success in a start-up environment. The R-squared value is 0.329,
which indicates that 32.9% of the variation in entrepreneurial success is explained
by the other exogenous indicators in AI revolutionary entrepreneurship. All the
loading factors of each construct are greater than 0.7, and the AVE score is also
more than 0.5. This means that all statements are valid arguments in a construct
and provide significant evidence for the path coefficient output.

Square Residual (SRMR) is approximately 0.08, indicating the model's goodness
of fit. The Normed Fit Index (NFI) value is not close to 1, indicating a moderate
fit of the model. For the bootstrap-based test of exact model fit, the original values
of d_ULS and d_G lie within the upper bound limit at both the 95% and 99%
levels of significance, which indicates a good fit of the model (Schuberth _et al_ .,
2023).

Table **9** presents the path coefficient results to test the hypothesis, showing the
original sample, sample mean, standard deviation, t-statistics, and the P-value,
which is significant at the 5% level of significance. This table examines the

**182** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **_Dey and Sharma_**

results to study the impact of OC, AER, FO, PI, and DM on ES in an artificial
intelligence revolutionary start-up. The original sample ranged from (0.077 to
0.213), sample mean highest at acceleration in education and research (0.210) and
lowest at financial outcomes (0.085), and a high degree of deviation was shown in
decision making and performance improvement on entrepreneur success, and low
variation at acceleration in education and research programme.

To test the results for H1, H2, H3, H4, and H5, we used t-statistics Table **9** with a
P-value that is significant or insignificant depending on the 5% level of
significance. In case of AER (t=2.870 & P=0.004) and OC (t=2.119 & P=0.034),
where the P value in both cases is less than the 0.05 significance level. That
means we have rejected the null hypothesis and support the H1 and H2. In
general, it is said that the acceleration in education, research, and opportunity
creation has a positive and significant impact on entrepreneurial success. In other
hypothetical tests, such as H3 (t=0.935 and P=0.350), H4 (t=1.726 and P=0.084),
and H5 (t=1.601 and P=0.109), the alternative hypothesis is rejected and the null
hypothesis is accepted because the P-value is greater than the significance level of
5%. Therefore, we conclude that decision-making, financial outcomes, and
performance improvement have a positive, yet insignificant, impact on
entrepreneurial success in an AI revolutionary start-up environment.

**CONCLUSIONS**

Entrepreneurial success is significantly fostered by AI, particularly through the
creation of opportunities and acceleration in research and education. These two
factors have a significantly positive impact on entrepreneurial success by
integrating AI tools into the entrepreneurial ecosystem, enabling sustainable
growth and innovation in the long term. However, other AI mechanisms, such as
decision-making, financial outcomes, and performance improvement, have an
insignificant and independent influence on start-up success in entrepreneurship.
This demonstrates that these areas are relevant, but their influence on
entrepreneurial success may need further exploration or more refined applications
in the modern digitized era. Overall, the study confirms that entrepreneurial
success is significantly influenced by AI-driven market exploration, research &
development education, and new emerging technology avenues, enabling
entrepreneurial success to thrive sustainably in the competitive techno-economic
scenario.

**RECOMMENDATIONS AND FUTURE STUDIES**

Our research outcomes reveal a significantly low level of awareness about AIdriven applications among entrepreneurs regarding their scope of entrepreneurial
success. To adopt and implement the emerging AI tools, it is recommended that

**_Future Entrepreneurs_** **_AI-Driven Entrepreneurship_** **183**

AI-specific training programs be integrated into the curriculum for management
students, trainers, and bankers/financiers. In addition, the study reveals that AI
tools are used more effectively and efficiently in a routine manner to create new
opportunities, explore new financial sources, and enhance marketing efforts
among entrepreneurs. Hence, a one-stop solution that caters to the all-around
requirements of entrepreneurs calls for the development of an Entrepreneurial
Utility AI. It suggests that policymakers and regulators give more importance to
opportunity creation, strategic decision-making, and resource optimization, as
these are significantly interrelated for entrepreneurial growth and development in
the circular economy. In the digital era of competition, new-generation
entrepreneurs leverage AI-driven techniques in their business operations for
strategic decision-making, data analytics, and security, unlocking unexplored
financial opportunities to encourage innovation, personal potential, and overall
economic development. This study makes a unique contribution by exploring the
future research importance of the level of adoption of AI tools across various
business environments, as perceived by different stakeholders. It focuses on the
long-term impact of AI on entrepreneurial success and operational activity in
specific industry prospects. A deeper understanding of this topic will be fostered,
as insights evolve into the importance of AI potential in entrepreneurial finance
and decision-making, and strategic pathways to achieve sustainability targets in
the changing technology era.

#### **CHAPTER 11**

### **Harnessing Financial Technology (FinTech) for** **Startup Optimization: Insights into Efficiency,** **Cost Management, and Growth**

**Sudin Bag** <sup>**1,***</sup> **, Kousik Mandal** <sup>**1**</sup> and **Nilanjan Ray** <sup>**2**</sup>

_1 Department of Business Administration, Vidyasagar University, India_

_2 Faculty of Management Studies, JIS University, India_

**Abstract:** In today's dynamic business landscape, startups face immense challenges in
optimizing operations, managing costs, and scaling efficiently. Financial technology

(FinTech) has emerged as a transformative tool, streamlining financial processes,
enhancing decision-making, and reducing operational costs. This study explores the
impact of FinTech adoption on startup efficiency and cost management, emphasizing
its role in fostering sustainable growth. Employing a mixed-methods approach, the
research incorporates qualitative insights from focus group interviews with startup
managers and CEOs in West Bengal, followed by a quantitative analysis of a survey
conducted among 112 startups. Structural Equation Modeling (SEM) was used to
validate the proposed hypotheses, revealing that FinTech significantly enhances
operational efficiency, facilitates data-driven decision-making, and enables costeffective financial management. The study confirms that startups leveraging FinTech
solutions experience improved customer experiences, streamlined financial operations,
and greater access to alternative funding sources. The findings highlight the growing
accessibility of FinTech tools and their critical role in ensuring startup sustainability in
a competitive business environment. The study also presents theoretical and managerial
implications, offering insights for startups to integrate FinTech solutions strategically.

**Keywords:** Data-driven decision-making, Digital financial solutions, FinTech,
Startup efficiency, Structural equation modelling (SEM).

**INTRODUCTION**

In the rapidly evolving business landscape, startups face constant pressure to
optimize their operations, manage costs effectively, and scale efficiently.
Financial Technology (FinTech) has emerged as a game-changing tool, offering
